Explicit `advance` still reports a required co-location. The opt-in `advance_routed` uses ordinary acknowledged transfers before advancing one tick. A cold bystander can move while unrelated warm solvers and their pending inputs remain on both workers. The next native step is to preserve an existing warm solver when a separated, cold dynamic body approaches it.

### Durable recovery qualification

The [two-process demonstration](../artifacts/multifidelity-recovery/qualification.log) saved a 1,784-byte file at tick 119 after the destination activated the contact group and its reply was lost. The supervisor killed the original coordinator with SIGKILL. The managed workers exited when their parent-owned seed pipes closed. The supervisor verified their old endpoints closed before launching the replacement. From the file, the fresh coordinator reconstructed the published source ownership, the pending 1.2 N·s input, and verified its journals. The following 81 frames matched an independently run, uninterrupted two-worker control bit for bit. The largest horizontal momentum residual was 2.22 × 10⁻¹⁶ kg·m/s. This qualifies explicit saves and same-build replay from the seed. Saving remains a caller decision, and replay cost increases with the saved prefix length. [Gzweb replay](http://127.0.0.1:8081/?backend=primitive&ui=full&lifecycle=1&replay=1&url=ws%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A9025%2F) · [Browser capture](../artifacts/multifidelity-recovery/recovery.gif).

```sh
RUSTC_WRAPPER= cargo build --locked --release -p gz-physics-distributed --bin physics_worker
RUSTC_WRAPPER= cargo run --locked --release -p gz-physics-distributed --example durable_recovery -- "$(mktemp -d)/recovery"
```

### Routing with a warm resident solver

A separated cold dynamic component can join one resident all-dynamic warm solver before its next interaction. The worker stages a clone of the native backend, appends the new boxes, and commits the result into the resident Bevy world. Resident entities, native handles and retained contact data survive the join. Geometry admission uses the solver's prediction distance. The qualified case supports one warm and one cold component, up to 32 boxes in total. Two independent warm caches still require a different merge operation.

The [routed contact example](../crates/gz-physics-distributed/examples/routed_contact.rs) moves box 3 from worker 1 to worker 0 at tick 36. The resident pair had already exchanged 0.900003684747 N·s. Box 3 first impacted it at tick 127, exchanging 3.592859143236 N·s over the fixture. A lost cold activation reply was retried while both workers retained the last committed barrier. Gzweb played all 57 frames through 2.200 s and Reset returned to paused frame 1. [Open the routing replay](http://127.0.0.1:8081/?backend=primitive&ui=full&lifecycle=1&replay=1&url=ws%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A9027%2F) · [Preview the handoff and impact](../artifacts/multifidelity-routing/routed-contact.gif).

A separate loaded test joined at tick 4, retaining a 0.1212 N·s cached normal impulse. The new contact and resident contact were simultaneously active at tick 60. The result matched direct insertion into an uninterrupted warm native solver bit for bit for 130 ticks. Comparing with a world seeded with all three boxes was exact when their native insertion orders matched. Inserting a lower global ID last changed the native handle order. Maximum differences against the all-seeded control in that case were 2.54 × 10⁻¹² across the sampled body components and 5.41 × 10⁻¹³ N·s in the normal impulse.

### Ongoing qualification and scope at 10:08 p.m.

A deterministic parameter sweep is running on a CPU-limited local user service until 3:37 a.m. PST, using pinned release binaries. It varies group size, geometry, mass and approach speed, compares native and partitioned controls, and attempts repeated loaded transfers and process replacement. The results distinguish a missing eligible second handoff from a numerical disagreement. [Measured results and pinned binary hashes](../artifacts/multifidelity-contact-sweep/20261119T0602Z) · [Representative gzweb replay](http://127.0.0.1:8081/?backend=primitive&ui=full&lifecycle=1&replay=1&url=ws%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A9035%2F).

Current implementation tracks are overlapping independent worker solves over Zenoh, opt-in predicted-contact promotion for articulated CPU models, finite anisotropic soft/rigid contact response, and oblique GPU contact with slip and spin. Each is being qualified separately.

### Overlapping independent worker solves

The opt-in `Coordinator::advance_overlapped` dispatches both admitted Zenoh step queries before awaiting their responses. It uses the same envelope, capacity, publication, exact-retry and fenced-recovery checks as the sequential barrier. A valid acknowledgment from either worker is journaled independently when its counterpart fails. A retry sends only missing requests. The transport keeps the original per-query deadlines and bounded reply channels.

On the Ryzen 9 9950X3D, five fresh release runs per size compared the modes over 300 measured ticks each after 16 warm-up ticks. Each worker held 1, 4 or 16 active contact pairs. The timing order alternated, and every measured body and contact frame matched exactly between modes. Median barrier latencies in µs were 98.8 → 55.9, 163.4 → 93.4 and 397.8 → 237.6. For 16 pairs per worker the p99 measurements were 641.6 and 407.9 µs. These are local loopback results during overnight development. [Measured latency and sample counts](../artifacts/multifidelity-overlap/latency.csv) · [Latency chart](../artifacts/multifidelity-overlap/latency.svg).

```sh
RUSTC_WRAPPER= cargo build --offline --locked --release -p gz-physics-distributed --bin physics_worker --example barrier_latency
target/release/examples/barrier_latency --pairs 1,4,16 --ticks 300 --repetitions 5
```

### In progress — bounded worker recovery journals

Current explicit recovery files rebuild from original seeds. To bound long-running worker recovery cost, the next distributed increment will checkpoint every native solver on a populated worker, including disconnected and singly owned bodies. Compaction must preserve native caches, ordered inputs, generations, retired IDs, tick and next sequence, and validate both replacement images before recycling either journal. The existing seed replay remains a separate verification path. Planned controls include mixed prescribed/dynamic seeds, separated contacts with latent caches, repeated compactions, loss around checkpoint capture, restart followed by reverse handoff, and comparison with an uncompacted solver.

### Three independently qualified contact profiles

- **Articulated contact prediction**. A translating prescribed assembly can name fixed or prescribed convex obstacles for a pre-solve shape sweep. The arm promoted at tick 541 with 20 mm of initial clearance. Every pose and twist component matched its always-dynamic reference through 1,200 ticks. Its held force delivered 0.012 N·s exactly once. The profile defers demotion of a spinning source. Rotation of an extended obstacle uses a conservative bound. Gzweb played all 51 frames and returned to the start on Reset. [Prediction run and tests](../artifacts/multifidelity-prediction) · [Prediction animation](../artifacts/multifidelity-prediction/promotion.gif) · [Gzweb](http://127.0.0.1:8081/?backend=primitive&ui=full&lifecycle=1&replay=1&url=ws%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A9031%2F).
- **Anisotropic receiver**. The XPBD cable transfers spatial impulses to a free rigid sphere with unequal user-specified principal moments. A separate native predictor measured angular impulse closure within 8.62 × 10⁻¹⁶ N·m·s across the contact fixture. Whole-system angular momentum drift decreased from 2.79 × 10⁻⁴ to 6.07 × 10⁻⁵ N·m·s as substeps increased from one to four. Final kinetic energy was about 1.297 J from an initial 3.15 J. The geometry is spherical and the receiver is centered. The real gzweb client fetched three GLB geometry assets, played 121 frames to 1.2 s, and Reset to paused frame 1. [Measurements and replay archive](../artifacts/multifidelity-anisotropic-receiver) · [Gzweb animation](../artifacts/multifidelity-anisotropic-receiver/receiver.gif) · [Gzweb](http://127.0.0.1:8081/?backend=primitive&ui=full&lifecycle=1&replay=1&url=ws%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A9033%2F).
- **Oblique GPU contact**. A Rust f64 Vulkan batch advances independent spheres on inclined immovable planes with vector gravity, rotation and tangential impulse. The qualification compared 2,048 lanes against the CPU and 64 representative worlds against a finer-step Rapier reference. Dense feedback on the final measured run gave 13.50M GPU and 15.95M CPU lane-steps/s. Gzweb played six lanes with body-fixed spin indicators through 600 ms and Reset. The GPU crate reached 98.17% production line coverage and 97.66% region coverage. [Timing, reference errors and coverage](../artifacts/multifidelity-oblique/final-qualification.log) · [Gzweb animation](../artifacts/multifidelity-oblique/oblique.gif) · [Gzweb](http://127.0.0.1:8081/?backend=primitive&ui=full&lifecycle=1&replay=1&url=ws%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A9037%2F).

The long sweep also found a tick-zero fixture refusal. Seed 2026117720 generated an initial gap of 0.0195655 m, inside the configured 0.020 m prediction distance. It remains in the pinned run's denominator, separately from a trajectory disagreement.

### Native disk restart and rolling recovery

A coordinator can now write two immutable recovery formats. A seed file reproduces the original run and verifies its full journal proof. The native format contains the complete two-worker barrier, including independent solvers, generation tombstones, serials, warm caches and globally ordered accepted inputs. The native form assumes the same solver build and a trusted local directory. Startup restores both real workers over private pipes and compares their complete read-back image digests before returning a coordinator. Both file formats sync the file and containing directory and refuse an existing destination. The original v1 seed fixture still reproduces its tick-119 journal proof. The v2 seed tests reconstruct the correct journal window after 14 compactions.

The release benchmark used two local Zenoh worker processes, one warm handoff, a pending impulse at the save point and five independent restores per format at each tick. Timings include opening the file and starting both workers. Each restored frame and its next eight physics ticks matched the uninterrupted control bit for bit. At ticks 150, 1,500 and 6,000, median seed-replay restores took 27.0, 198.8 and 964.0 ms. Median native restores took 6.9, 11.4 and 11.6 ms. At tick 6,000 the files were 60,663 and 16,758 bytes respectively. [Raw timings](../artifacts/multifidelity-native-recovery/latency.csv) · [Measured restore latency, median and range](../artifacts/multifidelity-native-recovery/latency.svg) · [Native restart tests](../crates/gz-physics-distributed/tests/native_recovery.rs).

```sh
RUSTC_WRAPPER= cargo build --release --offline --locked -p gz-physics-distributed --bin physics_worker --example native_recovery_cost
target/release/examples/native_recovery_cost /tmp/gz-native-recovery-trial
```

### Multiple simultaneous cold arrivals

One resident warm solver can now admit several separated cold dynamic components at the same barrier, up to the existing 32-body limit. All incoming bodies are staged in stable global-ID order before any resident ECS state changes. A loaded pair retained 0.1212 N·s in its cache while boxes approached from both sides. All three pairs contacted simultaneously, and every observation over 130 ticks matched direct insertion into an uninterrupted native solver. Two independent warm caches still cannot be merged.

Import preflight now performs the authoritative native padded-AABB intersection on each resident box without constructing temporary Rapier worlds for each pair. The control compared against direct native insertion over 480 rotated/translated placements and adjacent representable values at the prediction boundary. With 31 residents and 1,000 samples, median admission time for a clear import fell from 75.6 to 19.5 µs. Median time to reject overlap with the last resident fell from 68.5 to 13.9 µs. [Baseline](../artifacts/multifidelity-multi-join/import-before.csv) · [Updated timing](../artifacts/multifidelity-multi-join/import-after.csv).

The real two-process opposed-arrivals example routed both incoming boxes to the resident pair at tick 36. All three pairs contacted at tick 127, with a peak normal impulse of 6.454879885 N·s. All four bodies and all contact impulses matched an uninterrupted all-seeded native world bit for bit across 190 ticks. [Example](../crates/gz-physics-distributed/examples/opposed_arrivals.rs) · [Gzweb](http://127.0.0.1:8081/?backend=primitive&ui=full&lifecycle=1&replay=1&url=ws%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A9043%2F).

The 12-entry rolling-journal qualification crossed its original capacity 16 times and created 18 verified native baselines over 200 ticks. It exercised eight real worker replacements, both handoff directions, and a pending 1.2 N·s input. Every frame matched a third, continuously running two-worker control. Across three release runs, process replacement with five replayed requests took a median 3.74 ms, compared with 9.62 ms for 133 requests. Gzweb shows both worker journal gauges next to the same physics trajectory. [Full timings](../artifacts/multifidelity-compaction/timings.md) · [Gzweb animation](../artifacts/multifidelity-compaction/compaction.gif) · [Gzweb](http://127.0.0.1:8081/?backend=primitive&ui=full&lifecycle=1&replay=1&url=ws%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A9039%2F).

### Overnight regression checkpoint

A clean LLVM instrumentation build combined all distributed, multifidelity and Rapier tests with the articulation, promotion, rigid and soft scene fixtures. Production line/region coverage respectively measured 97.65%/95.10% for distributed, 100.00%/99.51% for multifidelity and 97.76%/97.35% for Rapier. The initial report from a reused coverage directory double-counted source mappings from stale objects. The clean result uses a separately captured source tree and a fresh target directory. [Clean report](../artifacts/multifidelity-overnight/audit-1-coverage.json).

The complete workspace release suite also ran against the source snapshot. Several existing C++ compatibility targets require a separately provisioned Gazebo/Zenoh native reference. A renderer device-stop test failed, and one server transport test stalled in the parallel suite before passing independently in 0.01 s. The workspace run also captured a regression test that still expected three-solver admission to fail. Its current version exercises the unsupported prescribed arrival, and the new dynamic-arrival test verifies successful simultaneous contact. The affected native physics gates are being repeated as subsequent changes land. [Full workspace record](../artifacts/multifidelity-overnight/full-workspace-audit-1.log) · [Isolated transport retry](../artifacts/multifidelity-overnight/standalone-server-control.log).

### GPU resident observation intervals

The new block API runs multiple 1 ms steps in device memory between observed barriers. It returns the final dense state plus per-lane contact count, first and last contact, minimum clearance, peak normal impulse and accumulated impulses. Over 4,573 ticks, 2,048 lanes matched repeated single-step GPU state and ordered impulse accumulation bit for bit. Relative to the directional CPU reference the maximum center error was 1.12 × 10⁻¹² m. Against 16 finer-step Rapier lanes, the largest center difference was 9.86 mm and the largest accumulated spin-phase difference was 6.37°. A failing intermediate step preserved the previous public endpoint.

At 2,048 lanes and 64 ticks per observation the RTX 5090 reached 147.39M lane-steps/s and the matched serial CPU reached 14.45M. Reporting every tick gave 11.69M on the GPU in the measured run. At only 64 lanes the serial CPU remained faster across the tested observation intervals. [Timing grid and chart](../artifacts/multifidelity-resident/measurements) · [Production coverage and device tests](../artifacts/multifidelity-resident).

### In progress — measured spatial load balancing

At a published barrier the coordinator can open a bounded measurement window on its two local workers. Each worker times complete native component solves. The wall-clock diagnostics are independent of physics observations and durable journal proofs. An opt-in spatial bisection can move one isolated cold body or complete active warm component per sample, subject to lifetime ID capacity, accepted inputs and a minimum projected reduction in peak load. The fixture starts with 12 contact pairs on one worker and two on the other. Qualification compares every barrier with an unchanged assignment and a monolithic native control, then measures transfer and steady-state costs.

### Measured load balancing and live SDF execution

Across seven release trials, the adaptive partition moved four or five warm pairs out of a 12:2 assignment. Six trials ended at 7:7 and one ended at 8:6. The median steady-state barrier saving ranged from 18.56 to 41.82 µs per tick. Total measured transfer times were 3.20–4.75 ms, equivalent to roughly 97–172 ticks at the corresponding median saving. Four trials improved p99 and three regressed. Two adaptive p99 values exceeded 1 ms. All seven runs matched the fixed partition and the uninterrupted monolithic control at every published tick. [Trial logs and gzweb capture](../artifacts/multifidelity-balance) · [Gzweb](http://127.0.0.1:8081/?backend=primitive&ui=full&lifecycle=1&replay=1&url=ws%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A9047%2F).

The normal Rust SDF loader and CLI now accept `--physics-engine distributed` and resolve a trusted sibling worker executable. The two local processes exchange authoritative Bevy/Rapier state at 10 ms barriers. The supported scene profile uses zero gravity and up to 32 homogeneous centered, frictionless dynamic or velocity-prescribed boxes. In the two-box fixture, routing completed at tick 75 and first contact occurred at tick 100. Reset reproduced all 132 frames. A native force acts across every internal substep. At 1 kg under 10 N for the first 10 ms, the measured displacement was 0.625 mm and velocity 0.1 m/s. Applying 0.06 N·m simultaneously produced 0.01 rad/s spin. Both endpoints of the 20 ms qualification matched a separate native solver in every component. [SDF, commands, measured results and gzweb capture](../artifacts/multifidelity-distributed-scene/README.md) · [Live SDF viewer](http://127.0.0.1:8081/?backend=primitive&ui=full&url=ws%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A9049%2F).

### Rotating soft contact, finite sphere pairs and fleet observations

A deformable cable can exchange impulse with a rotating finite cuboid receiver. The qualification resolved face, edge and corner contact against an independent Parry sweep over 96 three-dimensional cases, including 46 misses. Across 50 hits the maximum time-of-impact difference was 1.68 ns. The displayed coupled trajectory had two impact ticks. The four-substep run closed total linear momentum within 2.68 × 10⁻¹⁵ kg·m/s and angular momentum within 1.60 × 10⁻⁵ kg·m²/s. [Measurements, limits and gzweb capture](../artifacts/multifidelity-cuboid-receiver/README.md).

The GPU sphere-pair family adds two moving, spinning finite masses with friction. The dense fixture contained 1,792 impacts and 256 misses across 2,048 independent environments. GPU and directional CPU center positions differed by at most 3.82 × 10⁻¹⁴ m. Across 64 finer-step Rapier samples, different treatments of tangential impulse accounted for larger deviations, reaching 21.6 mm over the run. [Qualification, timings and gzweb capture](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair).

A stable ordered body-state lookup and translation epochs reduced the cost of copying native fleet state into Bevy. The prescribed-deck harness for 100,000 bodies reached 14.42× real time. The full 100,000-model scene with hierarchy and IMU refresh reached 0.595×, up from 0.356×. A separately initialized mixed workload with a preassigned active set reached 4.71×, up from 0.262× across 12.12 million public poses and twists. The long-term 100× full-scene target remains open. [Timings, fixture scope and gzweb capture](../artifacts/multifidelity-fleet/README.md).

### Normal scene worker supervision, immediate impulses and lidar

A scene now supervises failed physics queries. It identifies the exact affected local process, fences it, restores its acknowledged prefix and retries the interrupted operation once. In the live SDF qualification, worker 0 was killed at tick 55 and the cargo owner was killed at ticks 107 and 150. At tick 107 the scene had accepted a linear impulse for the next solve, an immediate spatial impulse, and a force and torque held over the substeps. All three interruptions completed without changing the physical trajectory. All 195 published frames matched a separate continuously running SDF control bit for bit. The measured recovery steps took 7.1, 13.8 and 13.4 ms in the recorded run. A separate 940-tick test crosses automatic journal compaction, tests retirement and checks native impulses at every step. Native spatial impulses use the resident world inverse inertia. A rotated non-cubic box matched direct native impulses across five different orientations. Native and seed recovery files both continued exactly after removing a contacting body with a pending input. [Example](../crates/gz-physics-distributed/examples/supervised_scene.rs) · [Gzweb recording and measurements](../artifacts/multifidelity-supervision) · [Viewer](http://127.0.0.1:8081/?backend=primitive&ui=full&lifecycle=1&replay=1&url=ws%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A9059%2F).

The distributed backend answers solid ray queries from the committed worker snapshots using the same f64 Parry ray primitive as native Rapier. A moving SDF lidar with 31 beams acquired 130 scans through ownership migration at tick 70 and contact at tick 94. All 2,704 finite ranges and 1,326 misses matched the native reference, including post-reset samples. Empty subscriber demand produced zero acquisitions. The Rust viewer displayed the measured fan and cleared the scan on Reset. [Fixture, operating commands and animation](../artifacts/multifidelity-distributed-lidar/README.md).

### GPU supported sphere pairs

A finite spinning sphere pair can collide while both spheres rest on the same fixed frictionless support plane in an invariant tangent-motion profile. The 2,048-environment run measured 1,792 pair impacts with both support reactions present. Each native support impulse agreed within 7.68 × 10⁻⁸ N·s and total external momentum closed within 1.80 × 10⁻¹⁵ N·s. Across 1, 0.5, 0.25 and 0.125 ms public steps, quaternion convergence was second order (1.9997). The finer three-collider Rapier comparison had a largest center difference of 0.293 mm in the common friction-law subset and 29.4 mm across all cases. The dense GPU path reached 5.75–7.58M pair-ticks/s in three whole-pipeline trials; the directional serial CPU reached 9.23–14.39M. All 24 device tests passed and production line/region coverage reached 97.80%/97.68%. [Measurements](../artifacts/multifidelity-supported-pair/final-qualification.log) · [Gzweb capture](../artifacts/multifidelity-supported-pair/supported-pairs.gif).

### Native solver splitting, gravity and fixed boxes

A worker can split a historical native solver into separated components. Each proposed cut must clear both the swept geometric guard and every native narrow-phase edge. The surviving pieces retain native handles, local solver caches and queued inputs. A complete 1–32 body component can migrate at a committed tick. In the three-box qualification the loaded pair retained its 0.020000063 N·s normal impulse at tick 68. Its detached neighbour moved workers, survived compaction and replacement, and returned at tick 111. All 125 advancing frames matched a continuously running native control. [Recording and qualification](../artifacts/multifidelity-split).

Distributed scene boxes now respond to uniform gravity, a held world force and torque, immediate spatial impulses and ordered step-start impulses. Free-flight routing includes directional acceleration and switches to a conservative all-direction bound after contact. Two boxes fell onto a prescribed platform at ticks 50 and 134, with an ownership change at 85. All 205 frames and the post-reset trajectory matched the native control. The native recovery profile preserves a prescribed body that falls asleep with zero twist. [Run and recording](../artifacts/multifidelity-gravity).

A true fixed floor supports two falling dynamic boxes. At 3.60 s the normal reaction divided by the public step was 29.429962 N at the floor and 19.619963 N between boxes. Each of the 360 steps matched the independent native solver. Native recovery format v4 identifies fixed, prescribed and dynamic bodies in mixed images. [Ordinary SDF fixture, reproducible commands and real gzweb capture](../artifacts/multifidelity-static-stack/README.md).

### Fleet hierarchy, rotating cable and heterogeneous GPU batches

The normal 100,000-deck SDF, including 400,001 public poses and IMU refresh, reached a median 1.614× real time across three interleaved runs, up from 0.560×. A 1,000-payload contact workload rose from 0.213× to 0.269×. All 141 lifecycle traces across model spawn, edit, remove and reset matched the original hierarchy path bit for bit. Automatic conservative dispatch can send a deck to native contact physics before a payload arrives. Its initial 100,000-deck fixture retained 46,474 analytical decks and dispatched 53,526. [Hierarchy measurements and replay](../artifacts/multifidelity-hierarchy/README.md) · [Automatic dispatch](../artifacts/multifidelity-fleet-dispatch).

A finite rotating cuboid exchanges impulse with up to four simultaneous frictionless contacts on an initially unloaded cable. Both particle contacts occurred between tick endpoints. The measured outgoing speeds agreed with the analytical control within 3.71 × 10⁻⁷ m/s. Reset reproduced all 120 advancing frames. The XPBD tests cover 96.44% of production lines and 96.58% of regions in this qualification. [Measurements and viewer](../artifacts/multifidelity-cuboid-cable/README.md).

Three GPU physics profiles can share one device batch with stable IDs and atomic publication. The 2,048-environment, 600-tick mixed workload achieved 7.94–8.23 million environment-ticks/s in steady runs, versus 4.05–4.15 million across three sequential GPU launches and 14.55–14.58 million on the serial CPU reference. Device frames matched separate GPU runs exactly. [Measurements](../artifacts/multifidelity-mixed).

### November 19, runtime editing, concurrent forced steps and loaded finite-box joins

Runtime spawn, removal and state replacement now run through the normal distributed SDF backend and gzweb lifecycle service. IDs survive reconstruction from either durable recovery format. Tests cover refusal before allocation, a newly spawned spinning body's impact, teleport after contact, monotonic allocation across deletion and reset to the original scene. A native control agreed over 265 ticks. Fourteen acknowledged browser-protocol commands and a 276-frame recording exercise the public workflow. [Recording, transcript and live editor](../artifacts/multifidelity-runtime-scene/README.md).

Two independently contacting finite-box solvers can now join before a shared impact. The joining solver remaps actual native bodies, manifold features, lever arms and warm impulses. A whole donor solver remains a single ownership transaction. Both loaded trains came under one owner at tick 85, struck at tick 204 and matched a monolithic reference for every body and impulse bit through tick 305. An additional interleaved input test moves full native groups three times with 32 accepted impulses, compacts each transfer and recreates both workers before checking the following 50 ticks. The coordinator preserves acceptance order across source and destination with a verified input slot mask. Capacity or invalid masks refuse before staging. [Loaded trains in gzweb-rs](../artifacts/multifidelity-warm-join/README.md).

Two independent worker steps with held forces and a uniform field can run concurrently. The coordinator retains an acknowledged worker result when the other reply is lost. Serial and overlapped retries, wrong-order or signed-zero inputs, and fresh-worker recovery are covered. At 1, 4 and 16 initially approaching pairs per worker, overlapping the field commands changed median end-to-end latency from 201 to 121 µs, 281 to 171 µs and 477 to 298 µs. Each workload combined five interleaved trials of 380 barriers per path. [Measured distributions](../artifacts/multifidelity-overlap/field-latency.csv).

### Distributed timestep and shared ground

Distributed SDF worlds accept 1, 2, 5 and 10 ms public steps. Across 2,376 two-worker barriers, every body pose, twist and normal impulse matched a continuous native run at the same interval. All four first registered the pusher impact at 1.000 s. Native images, action journals, empty workers and scene reset preserve the interval. [Qualification and 2 ms live viewer](../artifacts/multifidelity-distributed-steps/README.md)

Three sliding 1, 2 and 4 kg boxes settled on one shared plane represented in both native workers. The first support impulse occurred at 0.350 s, compared with 0.349749 s in free fall. A box transferred with a four-point warm support cache. The support counts changed from two and one to one and two, and the continuous native, migrated, journal-replayed and native-restored trajectories agreed bit for bit. At 5 ms the three final upward impulses measured 0.04905, 0.09810 and 0.19620 N·s.

Two supported boxes approaching each other transferred at barrier 50, joined their loaded solvers at tick 114 and first exchanged a box-to-box impulse at tick 400. Both floor impulses and all body states matched the independent native world over 470 barriers. All four contact points and normal warm starts on two yawed, unequal-mass boxes also matched after joining and restarting. The ordinary SDF viewer publishes the static plane as read-only and retains normal box lifecycle controls. [Numerical evidence, browser capture and live ground scene](../artifacts/multifidelity-distributed-ground/README.md)

### Isotropic dry friction on finite boxes and shared ground

The distributed scene loader now uses equal, finite, nonnegative ODE mu and mu2, including the SDF default. Each box and the optional plane carry their own coefficient. Native contact uses the smaller coefficient of a pair. Scene cloning, cold routing, native solver handoff, field stepping and both reset paths preserve the material. All-zero models retain their existing receipt bytes. Extended archives validate each finite box's coefficient and native combination rule. Legacy scalar plane elevation still decodes and hashes identically. Swept admission refines an overlapping world AABB only when a separating projection proves sufficient clearance between oriented boxes, allowing an initially separated slider above a long inclined surface.

On the 0.25 rad incline, both boxes contacted the ramp at tick 28. The 2 kg low-friction slider gained 1.2864291374 m/s each second. The other box drifted 6.095 × 10⁻¹¹ m over two seconds. Both normal reactions matched the analytical weight projection and all 600 frames matched one native solver bit for bit. A separate three-world test joined two independently sheared donors to a third solver, compared native point-level tangent and twist history through two restarts, and applied actual angular kicks during the 140-tick comparison. [Incline recording, numerical and browser evidence](../artifacts/multifidelity-friction-incline/README.md).

Two boxes initially supported on different workers by the same rough half-space came under a common owner at tick 263 and made mutual contact at tick 545. Every pose, twist, floor reaction and box-contact impulse matched a single native run through all 740 ticks. Another test recovered both the seed/action log and compacted native baseline with heterogeneous materials and four interleaved impulses still queued. Both fresh coordinators matched the uninterrupted reference for the following 115 ticks. [Ground-contact qualification](../artifacts/multifidelity-rough-ground/qualification.log).

### Folded cloth with swept surface self-contact

Rectangular XPBD cloth now tests nonadjacent vertex–face and edge–edge pairs through the substep, using barycentric effective masses for both material features. Triangles must begin and finish without interlocking. The original distance-only conservative advancement exhausted its search on a grazing fold at tick 167. Adding a separating-plane time bound resolved the same scene at the original budget. An independent native comparison covered 4,096 feature distances, 128 deforming impact times and 2,048 triangle-intersection decisions. The biggest distance and impact-time errors were 4.45 × 10⁻¹⁶ m and 1.22 × 10⁻¹¹ normalized substeps.

The loaded 27-particle sheet kept 30.000 mm clearance over the supported layer over 250 ticks. Switching self-contact off let it penetrate 108.612 mm. Maximum absolute structural/shear strain was 0.0639%. On the Ryzen 9 9950X3D, median release time was 0.095 ms per 2 ms tick with surface contact and 0.015 ms with it disabled. Sixty XPBD, eight scene and two example controls passed. The two new production files covered 429/438 executable lines and 876/888 LLVM regions. Real Chrome played all 51 measured frames to completion and reset to frame one. The admission limits, replay, measurements and commands are in the [folded-sheet report](../artifacts/multifidelity-surface-cloth/README.md).

### Force-controlled transport and release of a folded swatch

The ordinary SDF fixture `examples/folded_garment_transport.sdf` gives all 35 fabric particles finite mass and gives the swatch a 90° rest crease. The `garment_grasp --folded-transport` Rust controller closes two independent finite-mass pads, raises the sheet, translates the pads 55 mm and opens them. The frictional swatch rose 83.51 mm, travelled 42.922 mm laterally during the carry and dropped 103.10 mm after release. The pad reactions supported 17.182 mN against 17.168 mN weight. The last loaded pad sample occurred at 1.000 s, both skins cleared at 1.001 s and the sheet reached the table at 1.126 s.

The same trajectory with open pads left the sheet on the table. With closed pads and zero external friction, normal forces still supported its weight but the sheet travelled only 17.558 mm laterally. Relative material tangent travel increased from 1.375/2.042 mm to 31.676/38.759 mm at the two pads. An independent triangle check found zero cloth midsurface penetration and up to 1.069 mm overlap of a continuous 3 mm skin between particle samples. The release regression verifies the three physical trials, paused seed and reset, and checks the original flat-sheet capture. Real Chrome played all 71 measured frames and reset to frame one. The [garment transport report](../artifacts/multifidelity-folded-grasp/README.md) includes the raw 1,401-sample capture, replay, preview and commands.

### Distributed surface materials, population limits and native ordering

Finite boxes and the shared floor retain SDF isotropic Coulomb friction across warm handoff, both durable formats and reset. A paired incline matched analytical sliding acceleration to the printed 1.2864291374 m/s². A second rough-floor scene joined two independently loaded contact caches before impact. Across the two fixtures, all 1,340 complete states and contact impulse sets matched a single native world. The native oriented-box admission check also agreed with 12,000 independent Parry pair distances on every pair inside the contact margin. It separated 4,311 false positives from world-aligned bounds. [Incline](../artifacts/multifidelity-friction-incline/README.md). [Rough floor](../artifacts/multifidelity-rough-ground/README.md).

The SDF backend supports 256 boxes across two workers, with a maximum of 32 bodies in a connected native solver. A worker holding 256 loaded singletons restored a 3,518,114-byte durable barrier, transferred its last body with a pending input, compacted and replaced both workers. All 35 subsequent observations agreed with the untransferred control. A second regression compacted before 64 same-tick ownership transfers. All 128 bodies then matched through two replacements. Lifecycle admission now considers the next whole-world barrier, including accepted forces, before publishing creation or state edits. [Population, oracle and timing report](../artifacts/multifidelity-routing-scale/README.md).

A 256-box stacking scene matched all native isolated stack controls over 600 steps. One monolithic native world differed starting at tick 113. A separate native-only experiment reproduced the difference upon adding the 124th distant supported box, with the same target stack at identical coordinates. The discrepancy affects the deterministic monolithic comparison at large contact populations. [Full row-by-row control](../artifacts/multifidelity-routing-scale/native-contact-order.csv).

Across five matched 256-box SDF runs, lossless packed worker replies reduced 600 steps of application replies from 69.50 to 27.97 MB. The median tick fell from 3.209 ms to 2.167 ms, p99 from 7.710 to 6.849 ms. A separate spatial load-planning benchmark used real native work samples, checked 1,300 synthetic worlds against exhaustive placement and transferred a warm singleton in each real trial. The 384-body planning median fell from 382.40 to 218.01 µs. [Measurements and commands](../artifacts/multifidelity-routing-scale/README.md).

### Finite spheres and capsules across distributed workers

Centered homogeneous spheres can now cross ownership boundaries while spinning, under load or with accepted impulses queued. The normal SDF fixture placed a sphere and block on different workers and joined their loaded solvers at tick 268. The first sphere–block impulse arrived at tick 542. All 740 full states and both native impulse streams matched the uninterrupted single-world result. Rolling acceleration measured 1.500416 m/s² against 1.500000 analytically. All 1,200 sensor chords matched the native solver and differed from the analytical sphere by at most 3.11 × 10⁻¹⁵ m. A separate 430-step oblique sphere pair survived loaded migration and both forms of durable restart. [Live rolling sphere, measurements and commands](../artifacts/multifidelity-rolling-sphere/README.md).

Capsules carry a distinct centered Z-axis segment and cap radius through loading, routing, native mass properties and the v8 checkpoint. A horizontal capsule rolled into the block at tick 555. Its acceleration was 1.429837 m/s² versus 1.429319 analytically; 740 full frames and both impulse streams agreed with the native control. Tests also checked 18,000 seeded capsule–capsule, capsule–sphere and capsule–oriented-box admission decisions against native distances. Every pair within the prediction margin was retained. [Native checks](../artifacts/multifidelity-rolling-capsule/native-gates.log). [Scene qualification](../artifacts/multifidelity-rolling-capsule/qualification.log).

### Swept finite spherical pads on an external garment surface

The Gazebo SDF can opt a triangulated cloth into swept, two-way face, edge and vertex contact against up to four finite isotropic spherical fingertips. The normal manifold includes both opposing jaws, and the frictional material patch transfers angular momentum at the physical skin. A starting edge contact that detaches in one step is covered by a frictionless analytic regression and atomic refusal for unresolved tangential slip.

The folded-sheet grasp carried the COM 48.825 mm, supported 0.017304 N against 0.017168 N weight for 250/250 bilateral hold ticks, and dropped 78.94 mm after release. Independent whole-triangle skin overlap fell from the matched particle control's 1.069823 mm to 3.660 nm. The open pads left the sheet on the table. Off-center spinning impact and three freely deforming mesh resolutions cover impulse, angular momentum, energy and independent separation. Chrome verified all 71 measured frames and Reset. [Measurements, limits, SDF, controls and replay](../artifacts/multifidelity-surface-pads/README.md).

### Friction between swept material layers

A Gazebo cloth can opt into Coulomb self-friction on nonadjacent vertex–face and edge–edge contacts. Opposite impulses act at a common physical skin point and transfer the thickness-offset torque. Closed-shell geometry correction preserves momentum, and an obsolete-normal counterexample produced an explicit kinetic-work guard. Contact impulses and dissipation publish with each Bevy sample.

The matched folded-sheet trials reduced near-contact sliding speed from 0.548 to 0.171 m/s at a 1 ms soft substep, with a measured 30 mm inter-layer separation. The nominal response dissipated 0.01907 J and transferred 0.11250 N s tangentially. Twenty-four free-patch controls conserved spatial momentum within 9.16×10⁻¹⁶ in their respective SI units. Chrome exercised all 51 measured side-by-side frames and Reset. Scoped XPBD production coverage reached 95.47% of lines and 95.89% of regions. [Numerical report, SDF and replay](../artifacts/multifidelity-cloth-self-friction/README.md).

## Overnight distributed primitives and local worker pools

- Added centered solid spheres, finite Z capsules and finite flat-ended Z cylinders to the ordinary SDF distributed profile. Each retains its construction dimensions, homogeneous inertia, Coulomb material, motion tier and shape identity through packed observation, native transfer and restart. Existing box files retain their original layouts. Sphere, capsule and cylinder native solver formats are v7, v8 and v9. The shared plane supports authored orientation and offset. [Sphere](../artifacts/multifidelity-rolling-sphere/README.md). [Capsule](../artifacts/multifidelity-rolling-capsule/README.md). [Cylinder comparison](../artifacts/multifidelity-rolling-cylinder/qualification.log). [Oriented ground](../artifacts/multifidelity-oriented-ground/README.md).
- Compared independent rolling against flat-ground friction and analytic mass properties. All three 740-frame runs matched native full body trajectories, support and body impulses through a warmed ownership change. Sampled 1,200 strictly positive analytical rays per primitive. In the cylinder test a generic convex cast had a 1.12 nm maximum discrepancy. Intersecting the finite axial interval with the radial disk gives 7.77 × 10⁻¹⁶ m for the same measurement. Direct native distance tests exercised 24,000 rotated cylinder pairs with mixed primitive types, 18,000 capsule pairs and 16,000 sphere/box probes.
- A directional capsule support bound allowed 64 freely moving upright capsules in narrow neighboring lanes. All 32,000 body frames matched a monolithic native world. General angular motion or possible deflection continues to use the complete exterior radius. [Qualifying scene and animation](../artifacts/multifidelity-capsule-lanes/README.md).
- Added pool widths 3–8 to the normal Bevy SDF and CLI, global prospective routing, atomic multi-shard publication, per-worker generation fences, native journal compaction and fault recovery. Pre-step world wrenches, queued COM impulses and immediate angular/linear impulses use the full projected motion for admission. Four real SDF workers reproduced 1,100 native frames and ray samples, including recovery after SIGKILL. Runtime population edits remain a two-worker feature. [Four-lane viewer and commands](../artifacts/multifidelity-worker-pool/README.md). [Direct failure and input controls](../artifacts/multifidelity-worker-pool/protocol.md).
- Measured ordinary SDF performance at 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8 workers for 32, 128 and 256 boxes across five interleaved runs. All 22,500 whole-scene frame hashes matched. For 256 boxes, moving from two to four workers reduced the median of per-trial p50s by 18.4%. Median p99 rose by 2.7% under concurrent host load. [Five-trial results](../artifacts/multifidelity-worker-pool/scale-summary.md).
- Frozen instrumentation through the first pool and curved-shape implementation measured 97.06% of Rapier lines and 96.51% of regions, 97.18% and 96.12% for the multi-fidelity adapter, and 94.12% and 91.56% for distributed physics. The aggregate distributed gate remains open. [Coverage data](../artifacts/multifidelity-overnight/audit-5-coverage.json).

## Finite cylinder tabletop, rim departure and ordinary SDF visuals

- Added a normal distributed SDF with an upright fixed cylinder and a 0.6 kg sphere sliding across its frictionless flat face, falling off its rim and landing on the shared ground. At 1, 2 and 5 ms, every finite body pose and twist, finite pair impulse and shared-ground impulse matched uninterrupted native physics bitwise over 3,570 ticks. Native and distributed cap, rim, barrel and underside ray ranges matched analytical geometry within 1.12 × 10⁻¹⁶ m. [Loaded Rust control](../crates/gz-physics-distributed/tests/cylinder_tabletop.rs) · [SDF](../examples/distributed_cylinder_tabletop.sdf).
- The plateau load was 5.886 N at each timestep. The 1 ms step spread initial impact across two ticks. Measuring 20 ms impulse above weight yielded 1.447956, 1.447956 and 1.442070 N·s at 1, 2 and 5 ms. Free-flight height matched the native quadrature within 7.7 × 10⁻¹⁵ m, with continuous ballistic position errors of 0.548, 1.099 and 2.759 mm.
- Gzweb now displays authored opaque SDF cylinder visuals using bounded 128-segment GLBs with sharp cap normals and embedded diffuse color. Size varies per instance, and equal colors share a captured asset. A normal live session verified loaded support, flight, landing, Pause and Reset. Playback validated all 403 publications and both recorded cylinder GLBs. [Measured report and preview](../artifacts/multifidelity-cylinder-tabletop/README.md).

## Stack contact ordering and the measured support load

- Compared 64, 248, 250 and 256 boxes in ordinary two-worker SDF scenes against their uninterrupted native counterparts over 3 s, at steps of 1, 2 and 5 ms. The 248-box cases matched exactly. At 256 boxes, maximum position differences were 35.97, 47.97 and 75.94 mm. The 5 ms difference persisted at 66.57 mm after settling. Both layouts supported the correct load and settled within 1.274 mm of the ideal center heights. The 10 ms grid stopped before tick 23 when its swept bounds exceeded supported local grouping. [Results, limitations and plot](../artifacts/multifidelity-stack-mechanics/README.md).
- Isolated the native threshold with 123 versus 124 distant supported boxes and an airborne control. Additional inner constraint sweeps reduced 5 ms layout error to 25.75, 5.07 and 2.99 mm at two, four and eight iterations. At 2 ms the result varied nonmonotonically. Targets with 123 remote loaded boxes or 124 airborne boxes stayed exact. The release configuration retains four substeps and one inner sweep. [Native iteration plot](../artifacts/multifidelity-stack-mechanics/refinement.svg) · [Reproducible Rust control](../crates/gz-physics-distributed/examples/stack_mechanics.rs).
- Corrected native contact observations and the articulation support check to use contact points selected by the current solver step. On an otherwise settled 2 kg box, summing all retained geometric points reported 25.340291011 N against 19.619996817 N from vertical momentum. The corrected selected-point sum is 19.619996808 N. Every physical summary in the 12-case grid repeated exactly. Peak settled floor and interface load errors fell within 0.221 and 0.279 mN respectively. Active checkpoint round trips preserved caches, observations and 500 subsequent states. Older warm snapshots carrying a stale scalar fail native cross-validation. [Focused native regression](../artifacts/multifidelity-stack-mechanics/native-regression.log) · [Distributed restore regression](../artifacts/multifidelity-stack-mechanics/checkpoint-regression.log).

## Four-worker recovery, tilted geometry and controls found by the overnight runs

- A durable image now captures all pool workers, native caches, ordered accepted inputs and generation/tombstone history. The four-worker coordinator crash fixture loses an acknowledgement and its original endpoints, resumes on four fresh processes and matches 90 subsequent public frames. Cross-width, forged serial and duplicate history checks cover file admission. [Fault fixture and actual gzweb recording](../artifacts/multifidelity-pool-restart/README.md).
- The rolling finite-cylinder control matches 740 native frames, including both support and mutual impulse streams. Measured acceleration is 1.400523 m/s² versus 1.400000 analytically. Flat-endcap and barrel rays agree within 7.77 × 10⁻¹⁶ m. Continuous cylinder support and a certified separating plane admit 80 tilted thin disks on four workers with 32,000 exact body states. [Rolling drum](../artifacts/multifidelity-rolling-cylinder/README.md). [Tilted racks](../artifacts/multifidelity-disk-racks/README.md).
- The field campaign exposed a cold arrival whose accepted input could pin it away from an impending warm contact. The complete-solver transfer now carries that accepted input into the resident contact domain. Both failing seeds subsequently completed all 216 frames, two positive impacts and a killed-worker replacement. A fresh adjacent 10,000-seed campaign completed 2,160,000 exact frames with no error. Managed bootstrap now returns a bounded, identity-checked reason if the worker rejects its seed. [Corrected traces](../artifacts/multifidelity-field-sweep/corrected).
- A normal four-worker SDF exposes same-tick state edits of eligible free bodies through the gzweb lifecycle catalog. The native, ECS and browser controls cover stable IDs, immediate rays, a 9 N·s subsequent impact, worker replacement, pause and reset. [Qualification, interactive viewer and recording](../artifacts/multifidelity-worker-pool/README.md).
- Seven alternating paired planner runs per size, width and wrench condition measured 22.4–33.0% lower median routing cost at 256 bodies. At 32 bodies results ranged from 14.6% faster to 10.1% slower. [All distributions](../artifacts/multifidelity-worker-pool/routing-cost.md).
- Four matched public steps across 384 oblique sphere seeds reproduced every native frame. The continuous smooth-sphere impulse control exposed a p95 normal-impulse error of 1.225% at 10 ms and 0.154% at 1 ms. [Physical convergence and executable chart](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-refinement/README.md).

## Four-worker LiDAR packets, ray cost and final workspace gates

- A moving four-worker scanner published 260 genuine Gazebo LaserScan packets. All 8,060 returns matched the separately advancing native world through a target edit, loss of the scanner's worker, contact and reset. The hits included 560 capsules and 286 cylinders on foreign workers. Five beams changed immediately after the edit against a separate no-edit native reference. [SDF, recording, canonical packets and viewer](../artifacts/multifidelity-pool-lidar/README.md).

Before and after normal scene startup, a further 26,624 rays covered clipped endpoints, self-exclusion and inside starts at large coordinates. [Distributions](../artifacts/multifidelity-worker-pool/lidar-cost/README.md). [Boundary controls](../artifacts/multifidelity-worker-pool/lidar-cost/enclosure.log).
- A debugger isolated a clock-test shutdown stall to its own unread bounded subscription holding up the publication thread. Releasing that subscription before shutdown passed 25 consecutive runs.
