[Verification report](../artifacts/multifidelity-overnight/verification.md).

## Final long-running qualification

The loaded-group campaign finished with 26,086,909 advancing ticks, 179,581 migrations, 90,223 verified worker replacements and zero observed numerical differences against either control. Seven original poses did not pass startup admission and 865 cases had one eligible migration. The separate sphere soak measured 182,972 positive impacts and transfers. All 53 startup refusals in the warm-train campaign and both field-routing refusals were reproduced with their pinned binaries and rerun to completion on revised code. [Final counts, denominators and residuals](../artifacts/multifidelity-overnight/soak-results.md). [Six-hour summary and next engineering priorities](MULTIFIDELITY_OVERNIGHT_2026-11-19.md).

The 23 completed no-impulse train seeds were extended from 335 to 670 ticks with signed Parry surface separation and a momentum balance over the left train. All 15,410 states were native-exact. Three cases showed sampled penetration of 5.397–27.683 mm while the native control reported zero cross-pair impulse. The next contact-accuracy investigation should begin with these seeds and the independent momentum witness. [Per-seed observations and reproducer](../artifacts/multifidelity-warm-join/README.md#extended-observation-of-the-23-no-impulse-seeds).

The independent momentum check then detected 6.570 N·s in a positive contact control, matching the peak reported by the native solver. The final release check compiled all workspace targets. [Positive control](../artifacts/multifidelity-warm-join/momentum-positive-control/trials.jsonl). [Workspace check](../artifacts/multifidelity-overnight/final-workspace-recheck.log).

## Rotating-box contact refinement

Reproduced the three 10 ms misses at the pair level. A 15-axis cuboid test confirmed overlap, and the closest surfaces had already switched from closing to opening by the time the discrete solver saw them. Four seeds at 1, 2, 5 and 10 ms yielded 48,240 distributed frames matching the native control. The three misses all exchanged momentum at the finer steps. [Pair geometry, fixed-step results and viewer](../artifacts/multifidelity-train-refinement/README.md).

Implemented a constant-twist box contact forecast on committed distributed scene state. A regression with a spinning thin bar exposed a grazing impact missed by a native nonlinear cast. The forecast now proves separation over bounded subintervals before returning a negative on that path. It handles rotation, current proximity, edit/reset, unknown IDs and unsupported shapes. The native adaptive prototype used ten 1 ms solves in 14, 2 and 2 of 670 intervals for the three problem seeds, retained both impulse-free controls, and reduced their worst sampled penetration from 27.683 mm to 0.057 mm. Adaptive execution across distributed worker transactions and checkpoints is next. [Native forks, benchmark and limits](../artifacts/multifidelity-train-refinement/native-forks.md). [Adaptive gz-web-rs replay](http://127.0.0.1:8081/?backend=primitive&ui=full&lifecycle=1&replay=1&url=ws%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A9121%2F).

The independent sweep verified 11,691 initially separated, intersecting trajectories in both argument orders. A separate within-speed-limit thin-bar case started outside the native 20 mm prediction distance and struck at 1.5 ms. The raw cast missed it in both directions and the final forecast detected it. [Reproducible stress sweep](../artifacts/multifidelity-train-refinement/box-forecast-sweep.json).

Final gates passed 419 physics tests and four additional example tests, with four existing ignored tests. Strict Clippy passed across all targets of the three physics crates, and every default-feature workspace target compiled. [Verification logs](../artifacts/multifidelity-train-refinement/README.md#verification).

## Distributed adaptive contact refinement

Implemented opt-in adaptive box contacts with two through eight local workers. The coordinator chooses the common subdivision from committed scene state and ordered inputs. Workers perform one 10 ms solve or ten 1 ms solves inside one public Bevy tick. The journal records the decision. Per-pair and ground receipts include earlier microstep impulses even if a contact ends before the final solve. Warm migration and native checkpoints preserve both the aggregate and the last native solver state. [Implementation, API and live SDF commands](../artifacts/multifidelity-distributed-refinement/README.md).

Four real workers matched the independent native reference on all 2,010 public frames, including the three previously missed impacts and three separation controls. The loaded group crossed workers at tick 238, retried a lost acknowledgement, survived worker replacement and resumed from a durable file with a one-shot input pending. A separate SIGKILL test recovered two loaded contacts into four new processes and reproduced all 90 following refined frames. [Timeline and gz-web-rs replay](../artifacts/multifidelity-distributed-refinement/encounter.md). [Coordinator crash evidence](../artifacts/multifidelity-distributed-refinement/coordinator-crash/result.json).

Remaining work includes defining and qualifying automatic triggers for curved primitives and ground, and measuring per-island refinement against the current scene-wide decision on larger mixed worlds.

The final release suites passed 470 tests, with four ignored entries. The normal SDF path ran with 2, 3, 4 and 8 workers, and the new reader reproduced 90 frames from the preceding fixed-step release's checkpoint. [Verification](../artifacts/multifidelity-distributed-refinement/README.md#verification).

On the four-worker pusher scene, adaptive stepping refined 33 of 132 ticks and took a median 26.42 ms across six runs. Fixed 10 ms took 22.46 ms and fixed 1 ms took 189.41 ms. Distributed production coverage reached 95.89% of lines and 93.53% of regions. The 95% region target remains open. [Timing and coverage evidence](../artifacts/multifidelity-distributed-refinement/README.md#verification).

## 2026-11-19 — Curved primitive and ground refinement

Extended the distributed 10 ms adaptive engine to spheres, capsules, cylinders and mixed primitive pairs, plus the scene's oriented shared ground. Added swept shape and plane tests, a four-worker glancing collision with an analytical control, and an ordinary Bevy SDF where a sphere leaves a finite tabletop and lands on the floor. The glancing case recovered the analytical 32 N·s impulse and matched fixed 1 ms stepping bit for bit. Fixed 10 ms missed it.

Four real workers preserved a loaded sphere–capsule pair and a cylinder's ground impulse across migration, lost acknowledgements, worker replacement and restart from disk. All 150 public frames matched the native control, including all 91 after restore. A separate SIGKILL supervisor recovered a coordinator and four workers with 90/90 exact refined frames. The gz-web-rs replay includes 151 poses from startup through recovery. [Measurements, visual output and reproducible commands](../artifacts/multifidelity-curved-ground/README.md).

Next work is to scope refinement to interacting islands and measure the benefit in mixed scenes with resting bodies. The current decision applies scene-wide and conservative rotating-shape bounds may refine a near miss.

The four release suites passed 483 tests with four ignored entries. Distributed production coverage reached 95.95% of lines and 93.60% of regions. The crate-wide 95% region target remains open. The tabletop took a median 43.48 ms adaptive, 29.09 ms at fixed 10 ms and 308.04 ms at fixed 1 ms for 2.20 simulated seconds. [Coverage and timing](../artifacts/multifidelity-curved-ground/README.md#timing-and-test-coverage).

## 2026-11-19 — Island-local distributed contact refinement

The bare CLI option `--adaptive-contacts` now refines individual native islands. Use `--adaptive-contacts=scene` for the previous scene-wide behavior. Stable body IDs and committed subdivision receipts survive routing, migration and recovery. Workers support simultaneous coarse and fine islands on one owner and split departing bodies after native contact history and conservative motion bounds permit separation.

In the recorded mixed scene, all 135 ticks matched three independent native controls. All 83 mixed ticks had both rates on one worker. The encounter survived a loaded transfer, two lost acknowledgements, a worker replacement, and a checkpoint restart with one coarse and one fine impulse pending. A separate SIGKILL run restored four workers and matched 90 further ticks on all five bodies. A 67-body benchmark took 53.01 ms island-local versus 82.50 ms scene-wide (medians for 1.35 simulated seconds, eight paired rounds). It reduced per-body substeps by 81.13%. [gz-web-rs replay, timing data and run instructions](../artifacts/multifidelity-island-refinement/README.md).

The four release suites passed 493 tests with four ignored entries. Distributed production coverage was 95.83% of lines and 93.31% of regions. The remaining engineering questions are how to tighten the conservative separation bounds without losing contact continuity and how island scheduling performs in dense scenes where most bodies need the fine rate.

## 2026-11-19 — Earlier island separation and dense contact qualification

Implemented a Euclidean one-tick reach bound for deflectable bodies and a free-motion bound for ground-clear singletons. Before splitting, workers preserve both retained native contacts and impulses accumulated during the public interval. The force forecast now follows the native solver's arithmetic after an opposing gravity and applied force test exposed a missed collision. A 300 m/s glancing-impact test verified both the first and secondary collision within one 10 ms tick.

In the four-worker 26-body encounter, the loaded pair separated at 0.16 s instead of 0.77 s. Body substeps fell from 42,520 to 16,384. All 230 published pair impulses and 5,980 body states matched independent native controls at their selected rates. Migration, lost acknowledgement, worker replacement and fresh-pool restore preserved the loaded contact. A separate coordinator SIGKILL on two grounded pairs yielded 90 exact resumed ticks with continuous pair and floor impulses.

In eight paired release trials, median time for the 26-body encounter increased from 70.60 to 75.12 ms. The checkpoint grew from 105,459 to 243,164 bytes. Four continuously loaded stacks distributed across four workers also took longer, 41.80 ms versus 28.24 ms over 100 settled ticks. Both stack runs performed the same number of body substeps and produced identical trajectories and contact receipts. Next work is to profile native-world and worker overhead per barrier, then test batching or worker placement using these same workloads. The four release suites passed 500 tests with four ignored. Distributed production coverage was 95.88% of lines and 93.42% of regions. [gz-web-rs replay, live SDF, benchmark data and reproduction](../artifacts/multifidelity-dense-separation/README.md).

## 2026-11-19 — Native world cost and worker message batching

Profiled 24 free bodies at coarse resolution and eight loaded bodies at fine resolution. With body count and substeps fixed, the revised build averaged an additional 1.32 µs per coarse native world and 33.59 µs per loaded fine world per barrier (median paired endpoint increments). With four processes and fixed world counts, changing from one to four populated owners reduced mean revised barrier time from 213 to 193 µs for the fleet and from 429 to 282 µs for the stacks (medians across rounds). The controlled comparison covers 192 runs and 38,400 hashed barriers. [Cost profile and methods](../artifacts/multifidelity-world-batching/profile/README.md).

The coordinator already dispatches all worker requests before waiting. A baseline profile found that collecting replies, including remote solves, exceeded 1.1 ms at p95 in both dense scenes. Enabled immediate request and successful reply transmission and disabled latest-value consolidation on the private single-responder Zenoh channels. Eight alternating paired trials reduced median runtime from 75.57 to 58.48 ms for the 26-body encounter and from 46.90 to 21.13 ms for the four loaded stacks. The 95th-percentile tick time improved in all eight pairs for each scene. Physics trajectories, impulses, solver work and assignment matched. The loaded migration, lost-ack retry, worker replacement and full pool restart all passed. A separate SIGKILL test reproduced 90 subsequent ticks, and a pre-change native checkpoint reproduced all 112 following frames on the revised build. Both replay archives display the measured latency and were validated by the native gz-web-rs client. [Replay links, benchmark results and reproduction](../artifacts/multifidelity-world-batching/README.md).

The remaining measured cost is the serial overhead of many small, fine native worlds on one owner. A development candidate is to share per-barrier work across independent worlds on that owner and measure it against these controls while preserving individual refinement, impulse receipts and migration boundaries.

Verification passed 500 release tests with four ignored harness entries. Distributed production coverage reached 95.88% of lines and 93.41% of regions.

## 2026-11-19 — Parallel fine worlds within a worker

Implemented bounded parallel stepping for independent native worlds owned by the same process. The coordinator can grant one extra lane to a populated owner when the local CPU budget permits. The worker checks its post-merge topology and runs two disjoint batches through their ordinary Bevy schedules. Both finish before publication, with a deterministic first error and the usual recoverable barrier. Fine stepping now collects rigid and ground contact observations in one narrow-phase pass per microstep with reusable buffers. [Implementation and scope](../artifacts/multifidelity-local-worlds/README.md).

Sixteen paired release trials per workload used two separate four-CPU affinity sets. The median within-pair time reduction over 200 ticks was 27.3% for four fine worlds on one owner (13/16 faster) and 32.8% for sixteen (16/16 faster). The prior 26-body departing fleet improved 0.9% and the ordinary four-stack SDF regressed 0.8% on the same paired measure. All trial physics and placement controls matched. The controlled scheduling-only run with identical worker binaries improved four local fine worlds on one owner in 8/8 pairs. [Full measurements, controls and reproduction](../artifacts/multifidelity-local-worlds/README.md#paired-release-results).

Four independently loaded stacks matched 1,680 body states and every pair/floor impulse against four uninterrupted native controls. All eight impulses remained positive throughout 101 settled ticks spanning migration, lost acknowledgement, worker replacement and fresh pool restart. The replay stream matched the frozen-worker capture byte for byte. The earlier dense checkpoint replayed 112 subsequent frames exactly, and a separate coordinator SIGKILL recovered two grounded contacts for 90 exact ticks. The native gz-web-rs client accepted all 211 replay frames. [Visual and fault results](../artifacts/multifidelity-local-worlds/encounter/README.md).

Remaining work is to measure heterogeneous fine-world costs, especially when one large contact island dominates a worker, and use those measurements to evaluate how to split the work between execution lanes. The current policy splits by fine-world count and caps each eligible owner at one helper.

The four release suites passed 505 tests with four ignored harness entries. Strict release Clippy passed for the physics, scene and CLI targets, and the locked default-feature workspace check passed for all targets. Distributed production coverage was 95.92% of lines and 93.48% of regions. The 95% region target remains open.

## 2026-11-19 — Balancing unequal native worlds and contended helpers

A 25-body loaded slab world took roughly ten times the native solve time of a two-sphere stack. Workers now use body count, retained pair and floor receipts, and the upcoming substep count to estimate work. The helper gets a contiguous range and the calling thread advances the prefix and suffix. The coordinator gives scarce helpers to the eligible owners with the largest estimated serial workload. Exhaustive checks over 21,845 small layouts matched the best permitted split, and simultaneous faults in either lane preserved canonical error order and the prior published barrier.

Across 160 paired trials on two four-CPU sets, median within-pair time fell 13.7%, 13.1% and 14.2% with a single heavy world first, middle and last, and 30.3% with two adjacent heavy worlds. The equal-world control was 1.1% slower. With eight small worlds split 2/5/1 across owners and one spare helper, wall time fell 14.0%. The mirrored 5/2/1 control was 1.4% slower. The dense departing fleet was 0.5% slower and the loaded SDF was 2.3% faster. All physics and placement controls matched. Per-tick p95 gains varied. [Measurements, individual trials and reproduction](../artifacts/multifidelity-weighted-worlds/README.md).

The loaded 25-body world migrated as one island. Its 24 cargo contacts and its floor support stayed loaded through handoff, a lost acknowledgement, worker replacement and a full archive restore. All 8,190 body states and every reported impulse matched eight independent native controls. The frozen worker reproduced the physical trace and the gz-web-rs stream byte for byte. A separate coordinator SIGKILL recovered 90 exact ticks and the prior dense checkpoint recovered 112. The native gz-web-rs client validated 211 frames. [Playback, events and measured loads](../artifacts/multifidelity-weighted-worlds/encounter/README.md).

The next proposed phase is to measure workloads where the dominant island changes during the run as bodies form and release contacts. Measure how quickly the previous-tick cost estimate follows those changes, including the worst barrier and p95, and evaluate placement adjustments against the same recovery and equal-work controls.

The four release suites passed 508 tests with four ignored harness entries. Strict release Clippy and the locked default-feature workspace check passed. Distributed production coverage reached 95.96% of lines and 93.56% of regions. The 95% region target remains open.

## 2026-11-19 — Contact load changing between native worlds

Built two 62-body scenes that alternately launch and land cargo on two slabs. Each scene runs 350 public ticks with six releases and six landings. One slab took the longest native solve on 947/960 traced barriers where only that slab carried cargo. Added opt-in Rust worker tracing of actual thread assignments, prior and current contact counts, solver memberships and per-world solve times. Every one of 1,680 measured production splits minimized the estimated peak work in its permitted assignment set. The default worker changed its lane assignment 30 ms after every release and 10 ms after every landing. Rapier's zero-impulse contact receipts persisted for two ticks after release. [Fixture](../artifacts/multifidelity-changing-worlds/fixture.md). [Trace analysis](../artifacts/multifidelity-changing-worlds/lane-decisions.md).

In 16 paired uninstrumented trials per layout, p95 within four-tick transition windows fell 13.6% for adjacent slab IDs and 6.3% for separated IDs against world-count splitting. Over all 280 timed ticks, median paired wall time fell 16.6% and 8.2%. The single worst tick increased to 5.20 ms for adjacent IDs and 3.67 ms for separated IDs. Eight additional pairs per layout per alternative evaluated body-only and positive-impulse-only weighting. Counting only positive impulses reduced release response to 10 ms but beat production on event p95 in only four of eight pairs per layout. Body-only weighting had a lower event p95 and higher total time than the deployed policy on the paired medians. The production scheduling rule is retained. [Timings, controls and reproduction](../artifacts/multifidelity-changing-worlds/README.md).

A loaded 25-body slab migrated at tick 105, lost its launch acknowledgement at 120, and continued through a worker replacement at 140 and fresh-pool archive restore at 185. The recovery run exactly matched 21,700 body states, 10,056 pair receipts and 2,800 floor receipts. Seven further changes occurred across 165 post-restore ticks. The native gz-web-rs client accepted 351 frames and verified actual helper markers, both contact gauges, ownership and p95 bars. The previous dense checkpoint again reproduced 112 following frames and the coordinator SIGKILL test recovered 90 exact ticks. [Recovery replay and visual results](../artifacts/multifidelity-changing-worlds/encounter/README.md).

The next proposed phase is to measure how much of the slow landing barriers is spent in the native solve, transport and waiting for other workers. Align coordinator and worker spans for the same tick, then evaluate a targeted change against both layouts, including the equal-load control and the worst observed tick.

The four default release suites passed 509 tests with four ignored harness entries. The statistics and optional worker-trace regressions passed separately. Strict Clippy with tracing enabled and the locked default-feature all-target workspace check passed. Distributed source coverage with diagnostics enabled reached 95.99% of lines and 93.57% of regions. The 95% region target remains open.

## 2026-11-19 — Critical path and tail latency at touchdown

Added opt-in Rust cross-process tracing of coordinator submission, actual Zenoh callback readiness, ordered collection, worker handling, native world wall/thread CPU times and publication. On the five slowest adjacent-ID baseline landings the populated worker gated all five ticks. Its native batch averaged 1,183 µs. On the worst separated-ID touchdown an empty worker's reply took 3,872 µs to reach the coordinator in a 4,054 µs barrier. Receive waits overlap these spans. [Critical path and trace data](../artifacts/multifidelity-landing-tail/critical-path.md).

Changed the two-lane native scheduler so mixed worlds start expensive fine solves first and claim further worlds as each lane frees up. Equal or dominant work keeps a fixed split. Physics still commits after all work, with canonical fault selection. On 48 traced touchdowns per layout, mean native batch time fell 789→581 µs for adjacent slab IDs and 791→586 µs for separated IDs. The gap between the two lanes finishing fell 398→84 µs and 343→104 µs, respectively. Disabling worker-side transport batching and changing the helper's launch behavior were also measured. Neither experimental setting is part of the selected change.

The final three-way campaign rotated all six build orders across 36 matched blocks per layout on two four-CPU sets. Across 216 exact landing ticks per build per layout, pooled public p95 changed 2.174→1.644 ms adjacent and 1.696→1.688 ms separated. Worst touchdown changed 4.760→2.037 ms and 3.925→3.719 ms, respectively. The four-tick landing-window p95 was 3.5% slower adjacent and 7.4% faster separated on the median within-block comparison. The eight equal-world control took 5.9% longer on median paired elapsed time. Consistency of adjacent-ID window tails, OS thread wakeup and occasional late empty-worker callbacks remain open. [Timing results, alternative policies and reproduction](../artifacts/multifidelity-landing-tail/README.md).

The loaded slab migrated at 145, lost its launch acknowledgement at 160, and landed at 220 after the receiving worker was replaced. A complete restart at barrier 225 reproduced another 125 ticks and five load changes. In each layout all 21,700 body states, 10,056 pair impulses and 2,800 floor impulses matched independent native controls. The native gz-web-rs client validated 351 frames per layout with paired latency bars and measured critical paths. The earlier dense checkpoint resumed 112 exact frames and a separate SIGKILL test resumed 90 exact ticks. [Replays and event guide](../artifacts/multifidelity-landing-tail/encounter.md).

The four release suites passed 510 tests with four ignored harness entries. Diagnostic and statistics regressions, strict Clippy and the locked all-target default-feature workspace check passed. With the two diagnostics enabled, distributed source coverage was 95.78% of lines and 93.36% of regions. The 95% region target remains open.

The next proposed phase is to resolve scheduler wake latency on the populated worker and coordinator, using the existing trace to distinguish runnable delay from time spent processing network data. Evaluate any change against both exact-touchdown and four-tick-window p95, and retain the equal-world control.

## 2026-11-19 — Native helper wake and coordinator scheduling

Implemented opt-in Linux scheduler sampling on top of the existing cross-process barrier trace. It measures the same Bevy helper thread across solver-task submission and task entry and separates the coordinator FIFO reader’s CPU and accounted runnable time. The paired Rust benchmark report also breaks down exact-touchdown p95 by affinity and four-tick-window p95 by build order. The gz-web-rs replay shows both workers’ measured critical paths and helper dispatches next to the contact scene.

On the retained shared queue, the slowest traced touchdown in each layout waited 407 µs and 740 µs for the helper, including 402 µs and 735 µs of accounted runnable delay. The coordinator recorded zero runnable delay during its reply reads for those two steps. Sending an early Bevy wake hint before decoding the granted request shortened helper dispatch on the candidate’s own worst steps, but did not improve public latency. Across 36 matched uninstrumented pairs per layout, pooled exact-touchdown p95 rose from 1.200 to 1.240 ms for adjacent slab IDs and from 1.042 to 1.218 ms for separated IDs. Median paired four-tick-window p95 regressed 2.4% and 1.6%, and the eight equal-world control took 0.8% longer. The shared queue remains the production scheduler. [Benchmark, experiment patch and measurements](../artifacts/multifidelity-wake-latency/README.md).

Both workers in both layouts matched 21,700 body states, 10,056 pair impulses and 2,800 floor impulses per run through loaded migration, lost acknowledgement, worker replacement just before landing, and a fresh-pool checkpoint restore. Both also passed the 112-frame previous-checkpoint comparison and a separate 90-tick SIGKILL recovery. The native gz-web-rs client validated all 351 frames in each replay. [Replay links, event times and readback](../artifacts/multifidelity-wake-latency/encounter.md).

The next development question is why the worker can resume late after both native lanes finish. The worst traced separated-ID touchdown on the early-wake build spent 1.437 ms in that interval. Measure runnable time on the calling worker through lane completion and joining, then compare a change against the retained shared queue on both layouts and equal workloads. Separating network-runtime CPU from scheduling inside the submit-to-callback interval remains open.

Verification passed 510 default-feature release tests with four ignored harness entries, the focused diagnostic and example regressions, strict release Clippy and the locked all-target workspace check. Distributed source coverage with both diagnostics enabled was 95.68% of lines and 93.17% of regions. The 95% region target remains open.

## 2026-11-19 — Caller completion after the last native solve

Added opt-in Rust markers for both task bodies finishing, the calling worker returning from the Bevy scope and the end of the native batch. The analyzer uses the calling thread’s CPU and runnable-time counters where its samples support the interval. Earlier archives can still be read without those markers. The paired-trial report now estimates uncertainty by resampling whole matched runs within affinity and build order. The gz-web-rs replays include the two measured post-solve intervals and show when the CPU breakdown is available.

Tested running the caller synchronously inside the Bevy scope after submitting the helper. The experiment preserved each lane’s heavy-world reservation and canonical fault order. Across 48 uninstrumented matched pairs per layout, pooled exact-touchdown p95 rose from 0.986 to 1.102 ms adjacent and 0.986 to 0.997 ms separated. Median within-pair four-tick landing-window p95 was 3.1% and 2.1% slower. The equal-world control took 1.5% longer. The separated-layout bootstrap intervals span improvements and regressions. The production scheduler retains the existing shared queue. [Timing, uncertainty and reproduction](../artifacts/multifidelity-join-latency/README.md).

Eight instrumented runs per layout for each of three workers produced 288 exact touchdowns. On the shared queue the p95 interval from final solve to batch end was 14.6 µs adjacent and 22.5 µs separated. The largest inline-caller interval on a touchdown was 154.8 µs. Of that, 150.4 µs elapsed before both lane bodies finished and 3.9 µs from there to scope return. The prior early-wake worker was included to check the earlier 1.437 ms touchdown gap. That gap did not recur on the 96 early-wake touchdowns. [Scheduler attribution and raw profiles](../artifacts/multifidelity-join-latency/join-attribution.md).

Both timed policies for both layouts reproduced every body state and contact impulse across loaded migration, lost acknowledgement, worker replacement and a fresh-pool restore. The previous dense checkpoint replayed for 112 exact frames and crash recovery for 90 exact ticks. Both 351-frame recordings passed native gz-web-rs readback. Browser rendering remains unverified. [Visual results](../artifacts/multifidelity-join-latency/encounter.md).

Across the secondary sample of 13,440 traced steps, shared-queue scope returns exceeded 100 µs on 3 of 4,480 ticks. On separated tick 170 the caller was runnable for 1.244 ms of a 1.250 ms scope return. The inline caller also waited 2.355 ms runnable during a 2.360 ms return on adjacent tick 131. This identifies a scheduling delay outside the touchdown sample. The next proposed phase is to compare one populated owner with placing the two heavy islands on separate workers under the same CPU and process budget, including handoff cost, public p95, and recovery.

The four default release suites passed 510 tests with four ignored harness entries. The diagnostic regressions, strict release Clippy, standalone worker builds and the all-target workspace check passed. Source coverage with both diagnostics was 95.50% of lines and 92.98% of regions. The 95% region target remains open.

## 2026-11-19 — Process placement and solver concurrency

Compared keeping two cargo pads and six smaller islands on one worker with distributing them evenly across two owners. The same four peers remained live. With automatic helpers and 64 matched pairs per layout, median paired four-tick landing p95 improved 7.3% adjacent and 11.6% separated. Pooled exact-touchdown p95 was 0.988 → 0.981 ms and 1.027 → 1.066 ms respectively. Moving a loaded pad into a populated owner cost about 4.15 ms. Four moves to establish the split cost 6.26 ms. Added an explicit worker-local helper budget and committed grant observations to the Rust pool. With exactly two native solving lanes in each arm, splitting across processes increased total stepping time 8.9% adjacent and 6.6% separated. With helpers disabled throughout, splitting provided a second lane and reduced total time 28.7% and 27.8%. Each budget comparison used 48 matched pairs per layout. The production default continues to allocate spare CPU automatically. Both layouts supplied exact bodies, pair and floor receipts through transfer, lost acknowledgement, receiver death and fresh-pool restore. A continuous qualification fixture then found that its assumption of eight simultaneous positive floor loads ceases to hold at tick 8,451 under repeated cargo launches. All body and contact bytes still matched independent native controls on that tick. Preserved the initial trace and bounded subsequent fault-campaign epochs at 16 cycles (5,600 ticks) with fresh initial conditions before the next epoch. Identical final-step reply retries, cold placement and handoff transport are under development.

## 2026-11-19 — Confirm the final solve after losing an acknowledgement

The three- through eight-worker Rust coordinator can reuse original worker sequences to confirm an ambiguous final step. Four real-process regressions cover automatic colocation before the lost reply, queued impulses, signed-zero input changes, repeated loss and worker death. Across 192 matched lost acknowledgements per build and 67,200 checked ticks per arm, median retry times fell from 146–167 ms to 0.171–0.216 ms. Monotonic traces separated advisory request time, the initial physical solve deadline, cached final-step confirmation and reconstruction. Background probes now have a 25 ms budget. Supervised physical retries confirm a lost sequence for 150 ms. Across 48 matched permanent stops this reduced full-barrier p95 from 2.081 to 1.230 s. A 250 ms late thaw cost a worker replacement in all 48 short-grace cases. Across 72 public three-, four- and eight-worker faults, including loaded resets and fresh disk resumes with a held wrench, 7,200 Bevy steps matched ordinary uninterrupted controls. [Fault schedules, PID evidence and retained trace rows](../artifacts/multifidelity-final-confirm/README.md).

## 2026-11-19 — Moving supports, authored frames and a floating hinge

The moving rough-table solve reached rolling at 0.292 s against a 0.29125 s analytic prediction. Rotating the complete scene exposed a real 0.845 µm discrepancy at 2 ms. An independent native intervention traced it to the contact softness decision at signed separation zero versus +2.78e-17 m. The pinned production solver remains the reference for this qualification. [Moving-table data](../artifacts/multifidelity-rough-mobility/README.md) · [Native causal control](../artifacts/multifidelity-rough-mobility/native-branch/README.md).

A separately loaded authored world, including its mass properties and hinge axes, supplies the construction fingerprint. The image preserves root state, joint generalized state and pending effort. Through 8,192 ticks and 127 fresh native restores, internal state agreed exactly at each checked step. The independent planar dynamics solution measured first-order angle convergence from 5.70e-4 rad at 1 ms to 1.78e-5 rad at 31.25 µs. The finer step cost about 30 times as much public Bevy wall time. [Native image qualification](../artifacts/multifidelity-native-articulation-checkpoint/README.md) · [Physical error and compute cost](../artifacts/multifidelity-native-articulation-checkpoint/hinge-convergence.md).

A placement tape records every elected stay or whole-island move with the target tick, canonical membership and ownership generations. Actual native sequencing still handles impending collisions. Both changing layouts replayed all 350 ordinary barriers with zero timing windows and the exact original ownership trajectory. Fresh one-CPU workers replayed the retained four-owner tape through widths four to eight, including fresh restores immediately before each transfer. Added an on-demand reader for finite native manifold geometry on both bodies in Rapier and the multi-fidelity adapter. An immutable native pool or public SDF image can now expose the last native substep's contact points, sorted by stable public body pair and grouped by worker. Across 64 yaw-braking cases, 768 pre/post-fault image reads matched all point impulses and solver levers from an independent native run. ## 2026-11-19 — Coupled friction, ground patches, private hinge transfer and dense controls

The ground diagnostic now exposes the body's stable ID, outward plane normal, native contact lever, selected point and its normal impulse. A 36.87° inclined fixture inspected 157,952 points across 64 native cases and 12 ordinary SDF cases exercised two loaded moves, worker death and disk recovery with an authored torque pending. The combined immutable reader captures both finite and ground geometry while validating each native image once. On a loaded 54-finite-point, 14-ground-point checkpoint, median combined inspection time fell from 3.87 to 2.41 ms on CPUs 0–3 across 64 matched pairs. [Shared-plane qualification](../artifacts/multifidelity-ground-patches/README.md) · [Archive comparisons](../artifacts/multifidelity-placement-validation/README.md).

Combining sliding and yaw exposed a real limit of the grouped contact model in 2,808 of 5,784 loaded native substeps across 16 combined rough cases. Peak required work exceeded the pressure-weighted Coulomb bound by 56–64%. Returning to a separate reference at every microstep localized the difference to the friction law. Per-point Coulomb met the bound in the measured controls. [Inputs, witness and gz-web-rs playback](../artifacts/multifidelity-coupled-friction/encounter.md).

The experimental local two-process endpoint transferred a whole floating hinge and its pending effort. All 257 observed states matched the uninterrupted authored scene. Thirteen coordinator-kill scenarios and six additional worker deaths covered unpublished writes, committed decisions and lost solver acknowledgements. Across 288 final-binary transfers the median was 15.2–15.8 ms on ext4 and 0.65–0.70 ms on tmpfs. Production distributed joint admission and a lower-cost durable acknowledgement protocol remain open. [Private endpoint and limitations](../artifacts/multifidelity-articulated-handoff/README.md).

The dense regression comparison crossed both coordinator revisions with both worker builds in balanced four-arm blocks and retained all 640 four-arm blocks. The first pairwise analysis on CPUs 24–27 measured p95 increases of 7.0% for the dense encounter and 6.1% for the loaded SDF. The four-arm repeat measured +3.0% and −1.0%, with both uncertainty intervals spanning zero. [CPU layouts, ten scenes and all arm-level data](../artifacts/multifidelity-placement-validation/README.md).


The standalone `--physics-engine rapier --rapier-per-point-friction` option now reaches ordinary Bevy loading and loaded resets. The representative moving-table run matched the corresponding direct Rapier state at all 1,128 native steps. On a fixed SDF table the two final COM positions differed by 0.2161 m, and both models reproduced their own complete run after reset. A separate feasibility calculation found a single pure-spin substep whose entire measured wrench exceeded the corner cone set despite passing the direction-specific work test. The coupled fixture retained 2.35–2.41 J excess grouped work across nine timesteps. Native per-point execution cost about 11% more on two CPU cores. Distributed worker negotiation and model-preserving warm transfer remain to be implemented. [Production settings and tests](../artifacts/multifidelity-rapier-point-friction/README.md) · [All wrench constraints](../artifacts/multifidelity-coupled-friction/encounter.md).

The ordinary SDF benchmark then timed the complete 1,200-call Bevy stepping path in 128 counterbalanced pairs on each of two CPU sets. Relative median total cost for the per-point profile was +9.64% and +9.38%. The median within-run p95 changed from 7.03 to 8.16 µs and from 6.24 to 6.87 µs. Every arm was checked outside the timed interval against the retained profile-specific native result. [Authored SDF benchmark](../artifacts/multifidelity-rapier-point-friction/README.md).

In the independent stationary-table SDF experiment, the grouped and per-point block stopped at X = 0.8416 m and 1.1284 m. The offline reader matched 4,608 signed per-corner tangential impulses at the loaded fault cuts and eight more at the pure-yaw barrier to the independent native calculation, with each impulse inside its own friction cone. Across the original 320 matched timing pairs and a 160-pair repeat, per-point increased median paired elapsed time by 3.3–6.2% and p95 by 3.5–5.5%, depending on layout and CPU affinity. ## 2026-11-19 — A complete floating hinge on the production worker transport

The Rust SDF loader, Bevy physics backend, ordinary server and stock `physics_worker` now support one isolated, freely moving two-link revolute tree in a two- through eight-process local pool. Stable identities, full native link and joint state, ordered one-step torques and the current owner travel together. The controller stages the destination, fences the source and publishes the new owner with one synchronized atomic file replacement. Loaded reset discards held input, increments the simulation epoch and restores the authored pose. A failure to publish a reset is reported before the public ECS entities are replaced. Withholding four acknowledgements after execution preserved both worker PIDs. A stopped old owner retained its lease after its controller died and blocked a new controller until the process exited. The gz-web-rs replay covers 99 frames, and the live server recovered both visual transforms exactly at 192 ms. A repeat of the 14 physical runs with the final workers reproduced both trajectory digests. This shared-host repeat also measured a 62.78 ms p95 disk-backed advancing call, leaving durable per-step latency open. Four repeats per coordinator and filesystem yielded 5,580 timed intervals per condition. On tmpfs the mean duration fell from 202.53 to 146.39 ms and p95 fell from 236.16 to 197.51 µs. On ext4 the mean fell from 52.15 to 36.40 seconds and p95 fell from 133.16 to 64.68 ms. The two ext4 orderings measured markedly different gains, and the durable path remains slower than the 1 ms simulation interval. [Matched timing study](../artifacts/multifidelity-driven-hinge/cost.md)

All 468 selected scene and CLI tests, the locked workspace all-targets check, strict five-crate Clippy and the CLI check without default features passed. [Scene and CLI](../artifacts/multifidelity-driven-hinge/scene-cli.log) · [Workspace](../artifacts/multifidelity-driven-hinge/workspace-check.log) · [Clippy](../artifacts/multifidelity-driven-hinge/clippy.log) · [Feature check](../artifacts/multifidelity-driven-hinge/no-default-check.log)

The next physics phase should introduce a second independently moving sphere. Test when only one sphere needs the hinge's solver, when both contacts form one connected island, and how the island splits as the contacts drain. Loaded handoff, pending actuation, worker loss and restart should reproduce the uninterrupted three-role trajectory. Cross-host execution, crate-wide coverage and the cost of synchronizing every command to disk remain open.

## 2026-11-19 — Two moving spheres and a continuously driven floating hinge

The worker pool now hosts a Bevy hinge and two independently moving spheres. Stable native body slots preserve the surviving manifold as another sphere joins or leaves. Each free sphere initially has its own stock worker. During partial contact the hinge worker checks the next discrete step against the fully coupled graph, including all body states and five pair reactions. A matching resident step can be committed once without repeating the native solve. The SDF loader, durable session CLI, live Bevy drive, publication of both free bodies and reset support the new profile. [Worker design](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/coordinator.md) · [Native implementation](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/native.md)

In the forward run the two contacts transmitted 0.130390 and 0.009498 N·s simultaneously at 226 ms. The loaded island moved to worker 1 with both cached impulses and a queued +0.010 N·m. A left at 466 ms and B at 564 ms. Twelve runs covered both drive directions on three through eight workers. All 21,612 comparisons matched the uninterrupted ordinary Bevy run bit for bit, including the continuations after 12 loaded migrations, 24 worker kills and 36 whole-pool reopens. Dropping just the pending torque at the handoff changed the next joint speed by 0.00027989 rad/s. [Qualification](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/qualification.md) · [Four-worker receipts](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/four/summary.json)

The gz-web-rs replay opens at 226 ms with three owner markers and three measured impulse gauges. The native decoder checked every one of the 244 frames against the trace. A second view runs the actual continuously driven four-worker SDF. A private live server reproduced all four poses before and after Play, Pause and Reset. [Visuals, servers and reproduction](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/README.md)

The later sphere–sphere collision produced 0.393349 N·s of normal impulse at 1,292 ms, with at least 0.478 m of surface clearance from either hinge link. The scheduler conservatively put both spheres on the hinge worker. The next phase should give this pair its own worker and preserve the pair's loaded manifold when the hinge joins. Cross-host execution and reducing the cost of per-command disk synchronization also remain open.

The final partially joined layout measured 767 versus 539 µs p95 on tmpfs before and after reusing the verified resident solve. Its ext4 p95 was 171.60 versus 299.21 ms. Median total time for all 1,800 intervals was 0.386 versus 0.373 s on tmpfs and 57.81 versus 50.35 s on ext4. The frozen qualification compared 48 restart archives byte for byte and checked individual worker advancement counters on 21,576 intervals. [Timing distributions](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/cost.md) · [Frozen result comparison](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/optimization-recheck.json)

Twenty controller SIGKILLs exercised split advances, join and release, double-loaded handoff and pending torque on both sides of archive publication. Every restart recovered the expected owners, native image and disk bytes and matched four subsequent driven ticks. Ordinary scenes with unequal free-sphere moments of inertia or a bounded hinge retain their ordinary solver configuration. The worker loader rejects both unsupported constructions. Correctly checksummed cuts with contradictory resident observations, invalid free-sphere states or an inconsistent torque receipt are also rejected on admission. [Process crash evidence](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/controller-crashes-final.log) · [Ordinary compatibility regression](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/ordinary-pair-admission.log) · [Archive integrity cases](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/optimized-boundaries.log)

The final release run passed 746 physics tests across 127 executables, with 22 ignored by the standard invocation. All 468 selected scene and CLI tests passed, along with the workspace all-targets check, strict five-crate Clippy and CLI and scene builds without default features. The distributed production library measured 89.95% executable-line and 87.06% LLVM-region coverage. The release threshold of greater than 95% on both measures remains open. [Regression counts](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/verification.json) · [Release output](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/release-final.log) · [Coverage](../artifacts/multifidelity-two-sphere-hinge/coverage.md)

## 2026-11-19 — An independently owned sphere–sphere contact that joins a driven hinge

The previous run kept both spheres on the hinge worker even during their distant 1,292 ms collision. This phase adds a native two-sphere island, a worker transfer for that island and an archive format for saving its contact graph alongside the independently driven hinge. It reuses the ordinary two-sphere Bevy scene and the stock Rust worker. A new convoy SDF places the faster sphere behind the slower sphere so their established contact reaches the hinge. The previous phase’s archives, release binaries and physical trace remain available for direct comparison. [Convoy SDF](../examples/distributed_sphere_pair_island.sdf)

In the forward convoy the spheres share a worker at 125 ms and collide at 150 ms. Their applied normal impulse was 0.499975 N·s and the cached impulse was 0.029999 N·s. The pair transferred twice with the next torque already queued. It joined the hinge at 151 ms. At 168 ms the pair transmitted 0.417014 N·s while the leading sphere transmitted 1.377800 N·s to the hinge. In the original distant-collision example, both spheres now run on worker 0 at 1,292 ms while the hinge stays on worker 1. Both directions retained all 1,801 earlier physical states. The retained v1 archives from 226, 466, 564 and 1,292 ms also reopened without changes and agreed with every remaining ordinary state. [Convoy and distant replays](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-island/README.md) · [Migration matrix](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-island/matrix.json)

A 16 s comparison uncovered a native island-graph error at the second split. Rapier's removal notification for a disabled multibody tree was lost across serialization, and a collision-only update consumed sphere-disable flags before the persistent island manager saw them. The pair worker now removes the dormant tree immediately before integration, and the hinge's full step consumes its disable flags. The ordinary regression reaches 1,220 ms, including the 1,214 ms split while the A/B contact was still active. In the longer run the spheres themselves separated onto distinct workers at 5,594 ms. [Native checkpoint tests](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-island/native.md) · [Debug regression](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-island/convoy-debug-regression.log)

Across 3–8 workers and both torque directions, all 3,852 convoy observations matched ordinary Bevy bit for bit. The runs included 24 transfers of a loaded pair, 24 worker kills and 36 whole-pool reopens. Another 22 controller SIGKILL cases recovered both sides of archive publication during formation, handoff, merge, extraction and final release. In the extended four-worker run, all 16,001 states matched. The maximum measured impulse-balance residual was 4.44 × 10⁻¹⁶ N·s. [Release qualification](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-island/qualification.md) · [Controller crash receipts](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-island/pair-controller-kills-final.log) · [Extended trajectory](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-island/through-release/summary.json)

For intervals with steady pair ownership, tmpfs p95 increased from 259 to 353 µs in the convoy and from 338 to 478 µs in the previous distant scene. On ext4 the convoy changed from 47.11 to 53.18 ms and the distant scene from 65.92 to 19.27 ms. These figures compare the same simulated ticks over 24 whole-scene runs. Disk timings varied across repetitions on this shared host. The next performance phase should measure the extra worker round trip, checkpoint encoding and durable publication individually before changing the routing policy. [Benchmark and raw samples](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-island/cost.md)

The final release regression passed 752 physics tests across 129 executables, with 24 ignored in the standard invocation. The separately invoked legacy test passed on four real archived cuts. All 468 selected scene and CLI tests passed. Workspace all-target checking, strict Clippy on the five affected physics/scene/CLI crates and the no-default-feature CLI and scene builds passed. The final distributed production mapping measured 90.01% executable-line and 87.07% LLVM-region coverage. Both must exceed 95% before release. Cross-host recovery and general articulated contact graphs remain open. ## 2026-11-19 — Timing and reducing checkpoint serialization on independent sphere pairs

The last phase established correct independent pair ownership but raised tmpfs p95. This phase profiles each durable torque-and-advance interval. A separate Cargo feature records the four coordinator RPCs, their native solve and checkpoint work, wire receipt encoding and decoding, both archive encodes, and the file and directory syncs on each publication. The stock binary has the instrumentation compiled out. Matched comparisons use 25 steady convoy intervals and 539 steady distant-contact intervals per run. [Profile source](../crates/gz-physics-distributed/src/multi_sphere/timing.rs) · [Stock timing harness](../crates/gz-physics-distributed/examples/two_sphere_step_cost.rs)

The hinge and pair already produce encoded native graphs. Serializing those graphs element by element introduced extra work each time a worker sent a receipt and the controller published an archive. The multisphere receipt and installation types now use bulk byte buffers with the same fixed-width length prefix. The 192 KiB transport bound, durable torque acknowledgement and complete native checkpoints remain in effect. All 23,455 physical observations and all 49 archive images matched the previous build byte for byte, including the 16 s continuation and 30 worker kills. Both combinations of old and new v2 controllers and workers passed, including 60 alternating operator calls. [Codec and framing tests](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-latency/receipt-codec.md) · [Correctness and recovery](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-latency/qualification.md)

The gz-web-rs timing replay compares the previous and current median wall duration at each simulated tick. The Rust readback checked both time markers, three worker markers, three impulse gauges and four rigid-body poses on every recorded frame. The two sets of eight raw timing files independently reproduced all 320 per-tick median values in the replay. The live four-worker instance resumed from a verified loaded cut and passed play, pause and reset. [Replay, live server and reproduction commands](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-latency/README.md)

Across 40 whole-scene stock runs, steady-pair p95 on tmpfs fell from 272.59 to 204.00 µs for the convoy (25.2%) and from 381.15 to 304.25 µs for the distant scene (20.2%). On ext4 it fell from 171.56 to 67.40 ms and from 67.33 to 45.93 ms, respectively, with two repetitions per build on a contended filesystem. In the separate instrumented distant run, median controller decoding went from 22.41 to 2.72 µs, worker reply encoding from 14.49 to 3.84 µs, and the two archive encodes including checksums from 41.17 to 27.17 µs. Both builds performed the same four worker calls and two durable publications per steady interval. [Matched stock timings](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-latency/timing.json) · [Stage profiles](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-latency/profile.md)

Release and debug coordinators each passed all 42 process-kill cases with the final stock worker. Four retained v1 checkpoints matched every one of the 4,656 remaining ordinary observations. The optimized ext4 diagnostic runs still spent over 98% of their slowest steady ticks inside durable publication. The next performance experiment should add an explicit atomic torque-and-advance operation for callers that supply both inputs together, keep the independently durable queue API, and compare the resulting single publication with today's two. Its crash tests should prove that each restart sees either the pre-command state or the measured advanced state with the command applied once. Cross-host recovery remains open. [Recovery receipts](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-latency/qa-summary.json) · [Filesystem stage measurements](../artifacts/multifidelity-sphere-pair-latency/profile.md)

The release suite passed 753 physics tests across 129 executables, with 24 ignored by its standard invocation. All 468 selected scene and CLI tests passed, as did workspace all-target checking, strict five-crate Clippy and CLI and scene checks without default features. The whole distributed production mapping reached 90.06% line and 87.14% region coverage, below the greater-than-95% release gate on both measures. ## November 19–20, 2026 — Eight-hour development session

The session started at 21:17 Pacific. The first phases cover atomic actuation on a distributed world and finite capsule boundaries for soft-body contact in an ordinary Bevy world. The cumulative report records completed changes, visual output, measured qualification results and open work. [Overnight report](MULTIFIDELITY_EIGHT_HOUR_2026-11-20.md)

### Prescribed finite capsule contact for cables and cloth

Ordinary Bevy worlds now admit a centered fixed or prescribed capsule guide for XPBD cables and cloth. The solver follows the moving barrel and its two hemispheres through each substep and accounts for the reaction from axial spin and translation. A Parry raycast comparison covered 1,539 paths. The maximum difference in outgoing velocity was 2.01 × 10⁻¹³ m/s. Across four substep counts the crown cable landed at the predicted 246 ms. Its largest per-tick momentum residual was 1.23 × 10⁻¹⁵ N·s. The offset endcap cable's detailed trajectory remained sensitive to substep resolution. The cloth contact moved with the prescribed spin, reversed with it, and showed less than 0.020 mm of transverse drift with spin disabled. Reset reproduced each of the 500 cable ticks. Two gz-web-rs replays passed complete WebSocket playback, archive readback and native capture. Finite-mass freely tumbling capsules are the following phase. [Physics evidence, both viewers, and reproduction](../artifacts/multifidelity-capsule-guide/README.md)

The first overnight batch delivered the atomic worker-pool and Bevy operator paths on all four hinge profiles, 54 coordinator crash cases, and two gz-web-rs timing/contact views plus three profile views. The long trajectory sweep then exposed an integration failure on three inputs. Their preserved original cuts and geometry are under the overnight seeded failure directory. [Atomic publications and both benchmark layouts](../artifacts/multifidelity-atomic-step/README.md) · [Three-profile qualification](../artifacts/multifidelity-atomic-profiles/qualification.md).

Three previously failing 16-second trajectories now complete under the original rotation admission budget. Fresh three- and eight-worker runs agree for all 48 seeds. The original five-worker archive also renders and advances from its 6.676-second cut. [Reproduction, million-tick receipts and live views](../artifacts/multifidelity-root-drift/README.md).

### A freely tumbling finite capsule exchanges impulse with cables and cloth

Ordinary Bevy scenes can register an isolated native capsule for two-way soft contact with an orientation-dependent inertia tensor. Independent nonlinear shape casts matched all 192 tumbling paths. A 200,000-step planar reference identified continued loading after the barrel's first impact. Event resolution and refined continuation tracked that trajectory to within 0.193 m/s at 38.1 m/s. A cable striking the barrel and a cloth striking the end transferred −0.19231 and −0.26864 N·s respectively along their incoming axes. Both reproduced 500 ticks after reset. Linear momentum closed within 2.73 × 10⁻¹⁵ N·s. At four substeps the cloth's accumulated angular momentum drift was 3.57 × 10⁻⁴ N·m·s, predominantly in the internal soft constraints. The next phase addresses that discrepancy. Both gz-web-rs recordings passed full playback and independent transform checks. The production XPBD suite reached 95.67% line and 96.05% region coverage. [View both strikes and inspect qualification data](../artifacts/multifidelity-dynamic-capsule/README.md)

The initial 3–64 link floating-tree worker, guarded multi-joint inputs and ordinary SDF operator completed. The separate transport phase qualified moving contacts on loopback Zenoh and pipe/Zenoh resumption after controller death. Four-step-size ensemble mechanics and high-density Bevy publication now also have native viewer recordings. Full performance and physical limits are in the overnight report. [Nightly phase report](MULTIFIDELITY_EIGHT_HOUR_2026-11-20.md).

### Torque-free structural impulses in XPBD

Cables and cloth can opt into momentum-conserving distance and dihedral impulses. Two free rotors retained their entire initial angular momentum to roundoff over four simulated seconds. The original formulations retained 50.78% and 50.57%. Maximum particle trajectory errors against analytical rotation fell from 0.700 to 0.00505 m for the cable and from 0.628 to 0.00453 m for the cloth. In the dynamic endcap strike the whole-world angular drift fell from 3.57 × 10⁻⁴ to 3.77 × 10⁻⁷ N·m·s at four substeps. The remaining drift belongs to native rigid rotation. A pinned rotor, unequal-mass deforming sheet, fast angular motion, bounded numerical refusal and loaded-world reset have independent regressions. A single-core release benchmark measured 442 µs versus 256 µs for the 11×11 hard cloth. Four earlier capsule fixtures reproduced their archived data byte for byte. Both new gz-web-rs comparisons passed native playback, reset and independent physical-gauge readback. [Measurements, costs, viewers and commands](../artifacts/multifidelity-soft-angular-momentum/README.md)

### Isolated rigid rotation closes the remaining cable and cloth receiver error

The ordinary Bevy runtime now offers an explicit momentum- and energy-conserving integrator for an isolated COM-centered native rigid body. Six seconds of asymmetric rotation at 10 ms reduced maximum torque-free attitude error from 1.0423 to 0.0001203 rad, and driven error from 1.7752 to 0.0001015 rad, against an independent RK4 reference. The coupled cable/barrel and cloth/endcap scenes now conserve combined orbital and spin momentum to 1.51 × 10⁻¹⁴ N·m·s. A second native collider restored the complete native contact solve, and the original enrolled body resumed the isolated integrator after removal. Native contact, run-time impulses, reset and checkpoint admission have dedicated tests. The 10 ms torque-free Bevy loop measured 2.14 µs median per tick with ordinary integration and 3.32 µs with conserving integration. Both new gz-web-rs archives passed end-to-end playback, reset and transform readback. The next soft-contact phase handles prescribed capsule contact between the material vertices. [Results, visual evidence and reproduction](../artifacts/multifidelity-free-rotation/README.md)

### Finite prescribed guides support material between cable and cloth vertices

Continuous capsule contact now covers cable segment interiors, cloth face interiors, their edges, and both ends of an interval of parallel support. Simultaneous contacts on different guides share one normal impulse solve. Each load includes construction vertex IDs, nodal impulses, capsule region, original SDF slot and the support reaction. The 0.14 kg cable stayed at 0.720 m and transferred 1.3734 N through its segment. A 0.20 kg sheet transferred 0.981 N through each of two face interiors. Both particle-only controls dropped to 0.140 m. All 480 independent Parry geometry queries agreed. Removal preserved the surviving guide's original index. Swept bounds reduced the 11×11 cloth's four-guide landing median from 1943 to 336 µs and preserved both complete archives byte-for-byte. Both gz-web-rs comparisons passed native playback, reset and per-vertex readback. These are measured reactions on externally prescribed guides. The next phase qualifies finite moving receivers. [Scenes, costs, independent oracles and recordings](../artifacts/multifidelity-capsule-material/README.md)

The spinning assembly contact guard now predicts curved motion from the admitted native state, with 4,096 direct geometry and Bevy controls. Batched native readback, overlapping worker solves and positive late-epoch recovery each have real gz-web-rs evidence. An eight-link stress case uncovered substantial native momentum drift even with exact distributed replay. Independent compound, reduced and maximal-coordinate controls and the follow-on exact-lock work are in the overnight report. [Measured phases and outstanding work](MULTIFIDELITY_EIGHT_HOUR_2026-11-20.md)

### Finite two-way receivers take impulse between soft vertices

The actual tumbling native capsule takes the cable segment or triangular cloth impulse, including off-axis spin and simultaneous shared contacts. Analytical reduced-mass, asymmetric-inertia, two-receiver and high-speed cases passed. Independent geometry agreed on 189 hits and 171 misses. At four soft substeps, maximum total angular drift in both 0.7 s runs stayed below 6 × 10⁻¹⁵ N·m·s. Native final Z momentum changed by −0.17031 and −0.15540 N·s for cable and cloth. Each particle-only control transferred zero. A 16× finer public clock changed the cloth's post-impact trajectory by up to 19.45 mm. Both real gz-web-rs recordings passed full playback, reset and independent load readback. The next soft-contact qualification measures Coulomb friction between finite jaws and cloth faces. [Scenes, visual comparisons, convergence and cost](../artifacts/multifidelity-finite-material/README.md)

### Two finite capsule jaws carry an unpinned cloth through friction at its surface

Two 10 g free native capsules used ordinary world wrenches to pinch, lift, translate and release a 2 g cloth. At 55 mN closing force and μ = 0.65 the cloth moved 55.57 mm laterally, rose 69.11 mm and maintained bilateral support for all 121 held public ticks. The jaws carried 0.019648 N versus 0.019620 N weight. The 15 mN run lost the cloth and the 18 mN run held it, bracketing the 15.092 mN analytical threshold. Open, frictionless and vertex-only controls were unloaded during hold. Direct anisotropic endcap, Coulomb-disk, skin-couple and atomic pair regressions passed, including 600 independent curved native pair casts. Both 211-frame gz-web-rs recordings passed actual playback, reset and independent physical readback. The impulse-weighted hold slip was 0.184 mm/s, with short 23.62 mm/s excursions at public endpoints. Force-aware substep motion is the next phase. The native articulated tree and thick cloth now exchange normal and tangential impulses through their complete shared point response. Two-finger sticking begins at μ = 0.571469 in the independent Cartesian oracle. ### Sustained Coulomb friction on finite articulated supports

Four actual sphere pads now transfer normal and tangential loads to a finite-thickness moving cloth in ordinary Bevy. On a slope of 0.30 the true per-pad static threshold was 0.301003344482. A 400 g sheet with μ=0.45 held to 4.01 pm, while μ=0.20 slid 117.453448 mm in 500 ms. A tangentially launched cloth stopped after 36.326448 mm, and a separate uphill launch reversed. A deliberately asymmetric sheet drove the native table to 0.022036 rad/s and conserved free-axis angular momentum within 1.0 × 10⁻¹⁵ N·m·s. The real gz-web-rs clients verified 252 frames and an independent reader checked all 64,000 contact applications. Checked response reuse reduced median process CPU time 8.615→8.355 s across six paired runs. Static support has a known split-impulse speed inside each numerical substep. This ordinary solver qualification leaves distributed persistent-friction recovery open. Next is independent support of multiple folded faces on a single sphere and a convergence study of the oblique falling sheet. At 32 rollers and 513 nodes per cable, ordinary p95 fell from 108.60 to 20.27 ms. Group-specific pipe and loopback Zenoh leases now migrate and recover under a single Bevy publication. Two more campaigns retained 1,119 full-state comparisons across 30 route changes, 26 worker kills, two live stalled processes and 26 controller deaths. Current six-second stalled recovery motivates a measured warm-reply deadline. Three successful network-fault trials covered an active Pause, disconnect/reconnect, and a Pause before loss detection. All stopped at 151 ms with the exact reference checkpoint and four matching Bevy poses. An exhausted one-attempt trial remained at 150 ms. Once recovery was visible, the pending receipt took about 4 ms. When Pause preceded detection, it took 4.59 s. The native toolbar reproduced the pending and committed states across a 23.79 s outage. A second native trial verified that the terminal reason and tick stay visible after the coordinator exits and a reconnect fails. A Chromium WebGPU session independently clicked Pause across 24.09 s of packet loss and reached the exact positive-drive checkpoint at 151 ms. A legacy viewer continued through recovery to 157 ms. [Control traces and measurements](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-recovery-status/qualification.json) · [Browser during the outage](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-recovery-status/view/browser-outage/pending.jpg)

Next is explicit operator recovery after a terminal fence, with an archive-identity check before continuing. The network trials used two Linux nodes on a single physical workstation. Independent physical hosts and hardware failure remain open.

### November 20 — Twenty-phase operator recovery and controlled continuation series

The next series starts at the terminal fence left by the prior phase. The numbered ledger records its acceptance conditions across worker recovery, native and browser operator controls, simulation-time actuation, checkpoint diagnosis, deployment and replay. [Phase ledger and results](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-operator/README.md)

Phases 1–10 implemented offline inspection, transport-only recovery receipts bound to the originating session and marker, guarded pool reinstall, reconstruction of the paused Bevy scene, and a parked viewer endpoint with a single operator offer. The Rust CLI and gz-web-rs use the same admission protocol. A recovered viewer checks the matching origin token before enabling controls. A checked one-millisecond step reports the actual committed clock, including when a concurrent control changes the run mode. Tests covered competing operators, stale offers, a held writer, a changed valid same-clock archive, copied and replaced controller markers, nonregular files, and a directory sync failure after atomic rename. [Guard tests](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-operator/inspection/README.md) · [Control and viewer evidence](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-operator/view/README.md)

Phases 11–16 added held and interval-integrated linear joint torques, verified the pending command across recovery and epoch reset, and added physical and series comparison to the Rust checkpoint inspector. The seven-cut diagnostic first found different owner placement at 149 ms and different pending input at 150 ms. The first physical difference, at 151 ms, was 28.87 nm in position and 31.23 µm/s in linear velocity. For the recovery runs, nine guarded installs at 150, 154 and 160 ms yielded 57 exact matches against uninterrupted Bevy observations and independent native archives. Both local transports produced identical ramp results, and all 54 rejected step replays left the successor clock intact. [Measured drives and reproduction](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-operator/continuation/README.md) · [Checkpoint diagnostics](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-operator/inspection/diagnostics.svg)

Phases 17–20 exercised five bidirectional SSH outages around the loaded transfer, verified the real browser action, measured healthy control latency and exported a real checkpoint replay. Four deployed recoveries matched the complete next archive and all four poses. A substituted archive stayed inspection-only. The transfer preserved 0.499975 N·s of normal contact impulse. Six counterbalanced trials per arm measured median trial p95 of 41.96 ms before TCP_NODELAY, 21.83 ms with it and operator recovery enabled, and 20.00 ms with it and recovery disabled. All 18 timing runs ended at the same tick-222 archive. The replay deduplicated the three byte-identical reinstall cuts, verified 76 poses and radii and played 19 consecutive timestamps through 168 ms. [Deployment traces](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-operator/ssh/README.md) · [Timing plot](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-operator/latency/paired.svg) · [Replay and source archives](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-operator/trajectory/README.md)

A first fault pilot exposed an attempt to extract a scene from a simulation that had already faulted. The supervisor now retains the last healthy visual state and verifies its clock before serving it. A browser handoff pilot also showed why an unchanged scene at the same clock cannot identify the recovered supervisor. The coordinator now emits the admitted restart identity and the viewer verifies it. The final interactive demonstration leaves an eligible offer on port 9442, a recovered scene paused at 151 ms on 9443, and checkpoint replay on 9444. A separate process regression verified that a refusal from application scene setup re-parks the coordinator, rejects Play and step, preserves the archive and releases all worker leases. The qualified deployment used separate SSH and network namespaces on one physical workstation. Next work is independent-host qualification and handling supervisor loss, including reconstructing application-owned ECS state and establishing fresh operator authority. [Twenty-phase ledger and live links](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-operator/README.md)

The final Chromium click generated one request, received admission in 8.2 ms, and received the matching restored paused scene in 549.1 ms on this workstation. Fresh WebGPU and WebGL2 sessions rendered all four bodies at 151 ms. Both Wasm releases passed their 14 MB compressed budget. The CLI library passed 146 tests, the distributed library passed 145 with 58 marked ignored in that invocation, the five targeted physics integration suites passed 18 tests, and all four drive-example tests ran with worker-backed cases enabled. The viewer libraries passed 1,029 tests with one existing ignored case. The checkpoint, fault-injection, SSH, replay and browser qualifications were run separately. Simulator and viewer changes remain in their local working trees. Generated evidence follows the simulator checkout's existing ignored `artifacts/` convention. [Browser captures and wire evidence](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-operator/view/README.md) · [Verification logs](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-operator/checks)

### November 20 — Supervisor loss and cold recovery of a selected convoy checkpoint

This twenty-phase series implements recovery after losing the process that owned both the workers and the operator offer. It covers the durable physics cut and the authored drive installed in Bevy, offline inspection, a fresh operator decision in a replacement process, repeated process termination and browser qualification. [Numbered phase ledger](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-supervisor/README.md)

Phases 1–8 added a bounded, versioned recovery package containing the exact SDF, typed drive, native archive and origin of the existing Unix session. The Rust CLI supports creation, offline inspection and capturing a later cut from an explicitly selected base. The active supervisor can publish at startup or on SIGUSR1. The live path reads the installed Bevy resource, checks the clock, and compares the complete source against the healthy worker pool's retained bytes and original marker. A valid same-clock substitute that passes offline inspection is rejected as an active capture. A repeated signal at the same cut verifies the existing immutable result. [Source package and drive plot](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-supervisor/inspection/README.md) · [Actual capture receipts](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-supervisor/checks/live-captures.json) · [CLI and source checks](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-supervisor/checks/command-line.log)

Phases 9–14 rebuilt the ordinary Bevy scene before starting workers and added a cold operator path on the existing viewer protocol. Preview and live state agree for rotated and translated visuals, at a loaded contact, at construction and after an epoch reset. A fresh operator offer authorizes one checked installation against the reviewed digest, original directory, original marker, scene and width under the exclusive writer lease. A competing writer produced an inspection-only session. A temporary worker failure after cold admission produced a fresh offer at the same cut. A subsequent live worker-loss series reinstalled the workers three times, matched ordinary physics and exact native archives at all 19 measured cuts, and refused all 18 duplicate steps. [Origin and ECS verification](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-supervisor/checks/cold-attachment.md) · [Offers and refusals](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-supervisor/checks/competing-and-retry.json) · [Physics oracle](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-supervisor/checks/post-cold-oracle.json)

Phases 15–20 qualified the final binary in 15 supervisor SIGKILL trials. Five killed the original process with +0.015 N·m pending at 150 ms. Five killed it while the active worker had 48 unread RPC bytes; the surviving cut was 153 ms. Five exercised the migrated, loaded pair across SSH and Zenoh. All 30 sampled complete archives and uninterrupted physics observations matched, as did all 120 displayed poses. Median request-to-verified-paused time was 527.28 ms on paused pipes, 528.42 ms on interrupted pipes, and 805.08 ms on SSH. Chromium WebGPU performed the actual recovery on 9451; WebGL2 then rendered its verified successor. A separate interactive recovery remains on 9450 and a stale selection remains read-only on 9452. [Trials, timings and reproduction](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-supervisor/qualification/README.md) · [Browser results](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-supervisor/view/README.md)

The CLI library passed 150 tests, all four drive-example tests ran with actual workers, and both new command-line integration tests passed. The distributed library passed 145 tests with 59 marked ignored in that invocation. The targeted native-corruption, cold-attachment, worker-reopen, rejected installation and deployment suites ran separately, along with strict Clippy and the CLI build without default features. [Verification logs](../artifacts/multifidelity-convoy-supervisor/checks)

The two deployed worker nodes used separate network namespaces on one physical workstation. The recovery package restores the deterministic convoy drive and attaches to its original surviving storage. Independent-host qualification, storage loss, and explicit serialization of additional application-owned ECS resources remain open. Generated evidence follows the existing local artifacts convention.
