## 2026-11-23 — Supported parcel placement, first sheet impact and SDF GPU robot

The serial hand now waits for the named receiving shelf to carry at least 94% of a 200 g parcel’s weight for 30 ms before opening. The supported parcel settled by 0.106 mm and ended entirely on the shelf. Opening early gave 45.949 mm of downward travel and 101 ms without support. When the receiving shelf stayed parked, the gripper kept both jaws closed for 3,913 otherwise qualifying ticks on the pickup tray. All three reset runs reproduced their measurements and all 903 archived frames passed playback. The Rust example covered 211/213 lines and 383/403 regions. [Results and gz-web-rs controls](measurements/local-manipulation/serial-place.md)

For the two overloaded sheet meshes, guarded and unguarded runs reached their first plane impact at 265–275 ms after the disturbance started. The measured normal impulse ranged from 1.757 to 11.096 mN·s. Summing the individual particle momenta and the external jaw, plane and gravity impulses gave a maximum balance discrepancy of 9.2 × 10⁻¹⁷ N·s. The independent audit verified 560 native replay frames and 13,472 particle positions. Integrating further still encounters a later unresolved folded contact. The recorded first impact on the ideal plane is distinct from a landing on a finite tabletop. [Particle evidence and four replays](measurements/local-manipulation/fold-first-landing-view.md)

The ordinary Gazebo executable now loads a two-hinge robot with floor contact on the RTX 5090 from SDF. The maximum tool position error over 600 steps was 0.076 mm against a Cartesian reference and 0.250 mm against native Rapier. The first normal impulse was 5.34% below Rapier’s and all three released on tick 314. Seventeen focused tests passed, with 731/745 changed lines and 1,346/1,409 regions covered. The browser checked all 95 displayed transforms and shapes. [Source, measurements and replay](measurements/local-manipulation/gpu-sdf-tool.md)


## 2026-11-23 — Cloth impact qualification and finite receiving bench

The contact solver now checks the current material contact point after a ground impulse changes the velocity of a folded edge. A stale edge impulse that would add energy can only repair a genuine geometric overlap, then the continuous collision search runs again. The native tests include a captured 976 ns contact interval, a moving support’s normal and friction impulse, an already departed particle and a budget test that distinguishes a large candidate set from a difficult time interval. At 1.25 N the five-column guard advanced from the former failure at 294 ms to 361 ms before refusing a later cloth contact. At 1.30 N the seven-column guard reached 359 ms. Neither result establishes settling. All 167 XPBD tests and 71 robot example tests passed. The production solver and instrumented study each exceeded 95% changed-line and region coverage. [Exact coverage by file](measurements/local-manipulation/fold-impact-coverage.json) · [First contact in gz-web-rs](measurements/local-manipulation/fold-first-landing-view.md)

A second test used finite source and receiving benches with the same unguarded arm and 8.4 g sheet. The receiving bench measured 1.903061 mN·s at 265 ms, equal to the original large-plane reference. Moving the receiver away produced zero contact as the entire sheet passed 11.4 mm below tabletop height. The three saved runs repeated byte for byte, including the first impact frame. Seventy-three example tests passed with 165/168 lines and 365/381 regions covered. [Finite catch, miss and plane replays](measurements/local-manipulation/fold-finite-bench.md)


## 2026-11-23 — Recovering a withdrawing shelf and supporting cloth between nodes

The moving-shelf robot reclamps the parcel when the shelf withdraws during release and checks available clearance before placing it again. A 27 ms response kept the parcel supported throughout. At 28 ms it spent one solve unsupported. Sag crossed 1 mm between 35 and 36 ms. With the guard disabled the parcel fell 87.45 mm onto the pickup tray. Sixteen release tests, 29 response delays and three native replays passed. Changed coverage was 213/216 lines and 321/335 regions. [Response limits and three replays](measurements/local-manipulation/serial-withdrawal.md)

A 70 mm square support now carries a 140 g sheet even when every node skin remains over 106 mm from its footprint. Its first reaction occurred at 64 ms, matching a wide support. The narrow support’s initial impulse differed by 3.98% and its total impulse over 600 ms differed by 0.031%. An off-edge box carried no load. The previous finite pickup, catch and miss archives stayed byte-identical. The solver changes covered 193/202 lines and 381/392 regions. [Wide, narrow and missed supports in gz-web-rs](measurements/local-manipulation/cloth-face-support.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Receiving-support sensing, commanded GPU arms and glancing cloth collisions

The reusable Bevy receiving-support sensor measures directional impulse between a named payload and support. The 200 g parcel measured 1.962 N at both tested slice counts. Shelf motion, retirement, reset and restoration invalidated the release decision until a fresh dwell qualified. A 240-slice oblique impulse matched direct momentum change within 3.34 × 10⁻¹⁶ N·s. Fifteen sensor, trajectory and robot regressions passed. Changed production coverage reached 349/353 lines and 564/579 regions. [Sensor qualification and native replay](measurements/local-manipulation/receiving-support.md)

An SDF-authored GPU arm now accepts joint commands and publishes measured joint states through standard Gazebo transport topics. Against independent Rapier dynamics, the 600 ms trial stayed within 0.536 mm, released the floor at the same 311 ms, and produced 4.26510 versus 4.27002 N·s of total floor impulse. The first impact impulse differed by 5.9%. Reset repeated the measured run byte for byte. Additional real transport peer tests cover corrupted feedback, clock rewind, refusals and dropped connections. Changed runtime and qualification code combined covered 98.29% of lines and 95.58% of regions. [Commanded GPU replay and measurements](measurements/local-manipulation/gpu-command-state.md)

Finite box ridges now catch a cloth edge during oblique motion, including a complete 40 m/s crossing within one public tick. A 50 µm near miss had zero reaction. The oblique 40 ms hit measured 17.500 mN·s of normal impulse and 4.375 mN·s of friction. The solver covered 163/163 changed lines and 295/298 regions, and the recorder covered 88/88 lines and 203/212 regions. gz-web-rs verified 91 frames and 7,280 poses. [Glancing strike and near miss](measurements/local-manipulation/cloth-edge-strike.md)

## 2026-11-23 — A folded sheet sliding to measured rest

The five-column sheet reached sustained rest 1.588 s after the matched guarded grasp and 1.786 s after the control grasp. During steady sliding its measured deceleration was within 0.14% of the Coulomb prediction. The recorder captures the first real landing even when playback continues, follows the cloth in the camera audit, and requires 50 consecutive force- and speed-qualified ticks. The two replays passed independent momentum, geometry and particle checks. Changed solver code covered 29/29 lines and 68/69 regions. The recording command covered 85/85 lines and 157/161 regions across its unit and native runs. The seven-column control still reaches a self-contact refusal at 682 ms. [Slide, rest and both native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/fold-rest.md)

Five native transport test harnesses now check the actual routing or demand transition before asserting on the first delivered samples. The five complete targets passed all 23 tests in the instrumented Noble image, with 25 additional repeated scenarios. The aggregate qualification is open. An earlier hosted candidate passed the test suite and measured 94.47% whole-crate Rapier region coverage against a gate above 95%. A later candidate exposed module ordering under rustfmt, corrected alongside the replay audit. [Native transport receipts](measurements/local-manipulation/native-transport-readiness.md)

## 2026-11-23 — A complete pickup and transfer onto a fixed shelf

The lead-screw hand and universal wrist held a free 200 g parcel through every one of 4,001 commanded rail steps, then placed it 460.16 mm from pickup. The shelf carried it continuously throughout opening and retraction with 0.013 mm of settling. Open jaws left the parcel on the tray, and stopping 280 mm short of the shelf kept the gripper closed. All three runs repeated exactly after reset. Seven release tests passed, and the instrumented command covered 192/193 changed lines and 404/423 regions. [Pickup to placement in gz-web-rs](measurements/local-manipulation/serial-transfer.md)

Authored hinge limits also work on the GPU contact arm. GPU and Rapier hit the shoulder stops at the same 211 and 510 ms ticks. The floor and stop carried simultaneous load for 90 ms and the tool position stayed within 0.840 mm of Rapier. The first floor impulse was still 5.9% lower. The changed runtime and qualification covered 422/425 lines and 993/996 regions. [GPU joint-stop replay](measurements/local-manipulation/gpu-joint-stops.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Bounded refinement for the finer sliding sheet

The seven-column guarded and control sheets now both reach sustained rest. The control previously stopped at 682 ms. The new fallback retries only an unresolved time interval, with a shared budget, full reaction rollback, and no advancement of the actuator clock. Independent audits checked the actual first landing, 50 consecutive rest samples per trial, 11,228 displayed material positions and rewind. The first landing impulses and all recorded pre-impact positions matched the earlier references. The finer control also reproduced all 2,281 saved force and motion samples through the last previously accepted tick. The five-column physical archives and velocity traces repeated byte for byte. The 180 XPBD unit and integration tests and strict Clippy passed. Exact-object LLVM coverage reached 130/132 changed executable lines and 172/174 regions. Both instrumented seven-column archives repeated the physical traces and native frames byte for byte. On a pinned CPU the finer control’s first 681 postgrip samples had 63.4 ms p95 step cost. The full run had 51.6 ms p95, with the hardest individual step taking 18.5 s. The five-column browser camera changes also covered 33/34 changed lines and 81/83 regions. [Finer guarded and control replays](measurements/local-manipulation/fold-rest.md)


## 2026-11-23 — Publishing a fully observed fleet

Streaming adjacent canonical links in the Bevy scene reduced median model publication from 2.870 to 1.174 ms and p95 from 4.870 to 1.983 ms for 100,000 decks and 1,000 payloads. Across four independently compiled matched trials, median whole-tick cost fell from 5.186 to 4.656 ms. Whole-tick p95 varied with the benchmark and did not improve consistently. All 96,480,480 world/local pose pairs, 24,120,000 twists and 240 IMU samples matched. The change covered 32/32 lines and 102/103 regions. [Three benchmark methods and native replay](measurements/local-manipulation/fleet-canonical.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Whole-crate rigid-body coverage

The full owned Rapier crate passed its line and region gates on the qualified local profile. All 114 production source files mapped, with 27,821/29,051 executable lines (95.77%) and 45,798/48,149 regions (95.12%) covered. The 660 completed test executables included all 26 Rapier targets and produced 672 matched raw profiles. The new tests compare a tilted foot's two impacts against independently advanced time cuts and check loaded motion and checkpoint restoration. All-target, all-feature Rapier Clippy passed. The combined headless and renderer gates remain separate. The earlier hosted headless report also had gaps in the scene, CLI and distributed crates. Existing real-worker fixtures are being brought into the release run to measure the paths they actually execute. [Scope, counts and reproduction](measurements/local-manipulation/rapier-crate-coverage.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Refusing an invalid initial fold before local retries

A cloth whose authored skins already overlap can no longer enter the expensive local velocity retry. The 30 mm radius fixture faults on its first public tick, exposes no new energy measurement, and completes its release regression in 0.11 s. An instrumented release run took 1.44 s; its comparison without this check still had not finished after 460 s. Both seven-column physical archives repeated byte for byte. The new geometry check covered every changed line and region in the surface module; the complete admission change covered 24/25 lines and 42/44 regions. [Drop and rest qualification](measurements/local-manipulation/fold-rest.md)

The local humanoid's heading feedback is now checked against independent yaw calculations for planted, transferring and swinging feet. The hips respected signed limits and reset repeated the commands. All 76 executable lines, 148/153 regions and nine functions in the heading controller were covered. The ordinary CLI advanced 3,101 accepted 1 ms steps and ended on four loaded left-sole points. [Humanoid replay and evidence](measurements/local-manipulation/walk-heading.md)


## 2026-11-23 — Restoring the original warm contact under load

The stock physics worker reopened the original two-sphere checkpoint and kept its native solver bytes. Four bodies, hinge motion and contact forces agreed with the archived uninterrupted run through the next three steps. A one-ULP change to the stored inertia still failed validation. The crash-and-merge test and 159 Rapier unit tests passed, with 20/20 changed production lines and 25/26 regions covered. The combined port qualification started on review commit `84569c9`. [Measurement and reproduction](measurements/local-manipulation/headless-worker-coverage.md)


## 2026-11-23 — Parking the mixed-joint gripper between jobs

The screw-driven gripper parked before pickup, woke on its first jaw command, lifted a loose 200 g parcel 126.7 mm and placed it on a separate receiving plate. At full grip the public command gate and the native external-contact gate each refused demotion. The first jaw contacts matched the continuously solved robot's tick. The peak pose difference over the complete cycle was 0.897 mm. The gripper parked again after placement and a single wrist effort woke it on the next solve. Across three release runs the parked-window p95 fell from 612 to 5.65 µs, while complete-cycle p95 remained about 606 µs. Robots with moving angular speed-limit references remain dynamic until their complete native island can be parked safely. [Replays, checks and next step](measurements/local-manipulation/parked-spindle.md)


## 2026-11-23 — Resolving a sliding folded sheet from matched incoming states

At tick 737 of the finer mesh, the new solver took 11.32 s per public step compared with 18.53 s previously. The ground impulse error fell from 83.93 to 59.65 µN·s and the maximum particle position error fell from 21.55 to 15.46 µm against the 512-interval reference. The control still settled at 1.800 s, and its p95 across the full slide was 53.04 ms versus 51.60 ms previously. The changed solver covered 78/78 lines and 122/123 regions, and the timing recorder covered 4/4 lines and 12/12 regions. The exact inputs, Rust regression and both native replays are available in the [matched-state measurement](measurements/local-manipulation/fold-slide-refinement.md).


## 2026-11-23 — Faster continuous cloth contact with identical motion

The hardest sliding step took 9.20 s instead of 13.10 s in six alternated trials per version. Whole-run p95 fell from 53.04 to 38.24 ms in the seven-column control. Across two sheet resolutions and both grasp settings, 36 physical and replay files matched byte for byte. The two finer recordings passed browser and geometry checks. The changed contact search covered 70/70 lines and 132/132 regions. [Measurements and replays](measurements/local-manipulation/fold-slide-refinement.md#cost-of-the-same-sliding-contact)

## 2026-11-23 — Parking the complete speed-limited gripper

The capped screw and wrist now park their private speed references together with the public links and restore them on the first commanded or externally disturbed solve. Over a 10 s physical pickup and placement, both joints reached their limits while holding the unsupported parcel. The robot spent 750 ms parked, retained both loaded jaw contacts, and stayed within 4.80 µm of the continuously dynamic robot. An independent projectile test matched the first strike impulse and time exactly. An already-moving external collider defers capped parking because Rapier can batch physically disconnected objects. The remaining opportunity is to prove independent integration for distant moving bodies and relax that conservative restriction. [Measurements and gz-web-rs replays](measurements/local-manipulation/capped-spindle.md)


## 2026-11-23 — Driving a spherical wrist through the Bevy scene

The three-axis wrist now accepts torque in its moving parent frame. A free carrier took the equal and opposite reaction, with a maximum residual of 3.76 × 10⁻¹⁴ N·m·s across 2,400 steps. Reset reproduced every wrist sample, and the mounted and free cases passed. Combined coverage was 97.79% of changed lines and 95.02% of regions. The browser checked all 121 native frames and rewound to time zero. The screenshot verifier now preserves a capture named “initial” when it coincides with the first frame. [Results, replay and reproduction](measurements/local-manipulation/ball-wrist.md)

## 2026-11-23 — A compliant sole on a two-link welded foot

A floating fixed joint now carries the mass, inertia and pending loads of both links through the compliant lower-face solve. A tilted landing, an airborne shove and a second loaded shove matched a true two-box compound over 720 ticks. The aggregate COM difference stayed below 5 × 10⁻¹⁵ m. An unsupported upper-box sweep across the edge stopped before consuming the queued wrench. Reversing the native root, resetting and removing the plate passed. The next step is to solve an upper-link strike jointly with the compliant sole reaction. [Measurements and gz-web-rs replays](measurements/local-manipulation/compliant-weld.md)


## 2026-11-23 — A welded upper link striking a finite ledge

The new corner spring and the loaded sole now act on the same floating foot. The oblique strike delivered 2.302634 N·s, and the displaced ledge delivered zero. Reducing the time step cut the largest upper impulse error against an independent trajectory from 754 to 1.76 µN·s. Reset, moving supports and refusal of ambiguous edge or multi-corner contact passed with 98.26% changed line and 97.25% region coverage. Rigid frictional multi-point upper contact and a freely moving ankle remain open. [Measurements and gz-web-rs replays](measurements/local-manipulation/compliant-upper.md)


## 2026-11-23 — Checking stiff cloth grasps against both jaw loads

Two 2.8125 g fingers lifted an 8.4 g folded sheet with mass-scaled damping. With the stiffest qualified pads, eight native exchanges per controller update put the correct total weight on the jaws while the two individual forces pointed in opposite directions. Sixteen exchanges resolved both jaw loads to about 0.0412 N and the full recorded trajectory to 23 µm against 32 exchanges, at 5.12 versus 9.61 ms p95. All completed runs used the same 1 ms actuator clock. The solver also reports its actual accepted material intervals after uneven local subdivision. Exact native tests and examples at 238c269 exercised 171/173 changed production lines and 313/327 regions. [Measurements, reproduction and four native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/sheet-jaw-convergence.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Physical GPU release tests and independent coverage

The RTX 5090 passed 62 physics tests across 14 Cargo targets. Frozen build 84569c9 reached 95.26% GPU crate region coverage after exercising real hardware and capturing the resident worker's last contact on normal exit. An independent, build-ID-matched calculation also exceeded 95%. A new stock server recording matched all 600 earlier robot observations, and gz-web-rs verified 34 frames. Shared scene and CLI qualification remains underway. [Hardware evidence and new replay](measurements/local-manipulation/gpu-hardware-qualification.md)


## 2026-11-23 — Ground load through a freely moving ankle

The native ankle bent 19.973° under a 0.55 N·m shank moment while the same robot with a locked ankle held zero. The independently integrated trajectory predicted 19.968°, and the sole stayed loaded throughout both runs. Halving the internal step reduced the free ankle’s maximum transmitted impulse error from 1.328 to 0.660 µN·s. Reset, finite-edge refusal, held joint effort and zero duplicate rigid impulse passed with 97.48% changed line coverage and 95.47% region coverage. Rigid frictional multi-point shank contact remains open. [Measurements and gz-web-rs replays](measurements/local-manipulation/compliant-ankle.md)

## 2026-11-23 — The GPU tool's first impact and the rest of its landing tick

Matching the incoming arm state at 64 ms showed that the first impact and the remaining supported interval made separate contributions to the reaction. Resolving touchdown inside the existing public tick reduced the 1 ms impulse error against independent Cartesian dynamics from 5.51 to 0.030 mN·s. Across 2,048 arms the complete step cost rose from 446 to 451 µs p95. The independent native Rapier landing still differs by 4.17% for the live arm. Reset reproduced its 600 hardware samples. [Matched-state experiment, costs and native gz-web-rs replay](measurements/local-manipulation/gpu-tool-impact.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Whole-workspace release check on the fleet publication fix

The 8c57ff6 headless build passed all 3,727 ordinary tests and 39 required physical worker cases, including the trajectory with 16 worker crashes. The release check then exited on insufficient CLI coverage. Region coverage was 88.933% for the CLI, 90.993% for the scene and 81.895% for the distributed crate. Rapier, multi-fidelity, XPBD and server all cleared 95% on this same headless build. The frozen renderer run and broader physical CLI and scene coverage are in progress. The later live universal-wrist fix and GPU qualification are on the integration review branch. [Local operator](measurements/local-manipulation/local-robot-operator.md) · [Headless physical checks](measurements/local-manipulation/headless-worker-coverage.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Transferring a free cable with the serial gripper

The serial screw and wrist lifted a free 22 g cable 186 mm, cleared the barrier by 37.6 mm, and released it onto a shelf carrying its full 0.21582 N weight. The two pads carried 0.09747 N and 0.11833 N during travel. Open jaws left the cable on the pickup table, and a shorter stroke struck the barrier. All 14,000 ticks per trial matched after reset, and changed production coverage reached 412/418 lines and 819/848 regions. The remaining controller work is detecting a snag and changing the route while the cable is in the gripper. [Measurements and three gz-web-rs replays](measurements/local-manipulation/serial-cable-transfer.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Sliding a freely hinged foot off a deck edge

The toe overhung the finite deck by 28.89 mm while its coated sole stayed loaded. The shank struck the deck at 32.375 ms, within 55 µs of an independent reference, and shortening the shank produced zero rigid impulse. The displayed trajectory stayed within 8.23 µm of the reference. Refining the native contact steps reduced the trajectory error to 2.06 µm, although the peak cumulative impulse error increased on the finest grid. Frictional shank strikes and inward sidewall crossings remain open. [Measurements, coverage and three native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/compliant-ankle-edge.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Clearing a loaded shelf edge during serial transfer

The 400 g parcel that jammed under 13.25 N of shelf reaction now clears the edge by 19.77 mm and settles at its measured 3.924 N weight. All 57 cold/reset trials reproduced every recorded pose. The 12 N overloads withheld release. The 8 N transfer succeeded with 0.5, 1 and 2 ms physics, although opening times differed by 4.85 seconds. Changed production coverage reached 99.30% of lines and 96.09% of regions. [Measured loads, timings and four native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/serial-pressure.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Consistent release of a parcel over a shelf edge

Keeping the parcel's center of mass over the shelf before set-down narrowed the 8 N release-time spread from 4.85 seconds to 134 ms across 0.5, 1 and 2 ms physics. The 1 and 2 ms trajectories came within 0.73 and 2.73 mm of the 0.5 ms run, formerly 37.75 and 45.76 mm. A finer 0.25 ms holdout released 408 ms later than the 0.5 ms run. All 16 resets reproduced their trajectories, and every 12 N trial withheld opening after losing its grasp. Changed production coverage reached 98.94% of lines and 97.65% of regions. [Matched native impulses, force convergence and three replays](measurements/local-manipulation/serial-convergence.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Checking the native arm's rebound from a matched landing

Four native stabilization passes reduced the unwanted upward velocity at the end of the landing tick from 56.38 to 0.0121 mm/s. The GPU and independent native impulses then agreed within 0.00335% from the same incoming state. A 600 ms continuous replay shows all nine native arms touching at 65 ms and unloading after 307 ms. The study keeps the ordinary runtime defaults. [Measurements and gz-web-rs comparison](measurements/local-manipulation/native-landing-passes.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Resolving self-contact inside a stiff folded-sheet grasp

The stiff spring jaws now continue past the 640 ms pickup state where a closing cloth edge used to stop the simulation. Six exchange rates completed the full pickup and release with the same actuator clock. The measured force split between the two jaws still varies considerably, so this extreme compliance remains outside the qualified range. The exact failed state passed at four material resolutions, all 190 XPBD tests passed and the solver change reached 100% executable line and region coverage. [Signed forces, native replays and regression](measurements/local-manipulation/spring-pad-endpoint.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Restoring the rigid gripper's tactile readings

Rigid fingertips now report the normal and friction impulse on each cloth particle. Both jaws measured 0.825 N of squeeze and shared the sheet weight, and the existing motion and replay stayed byte-identical. A twelve-rate comparison against 1,000 exchanges per millisecond needed 500 to place every recorded node within 0.1 mm. At that setting each simulated millisecond cost 131 ms p95 on the shared host. This is a measured accuracy limit for the light, rigid jaws. [Forces, timings, coverage and replays](measurements/local-manipulation/hard-jaw-tactile.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Recovering a cable snag during serial transport

A 0.447 N contact triggered braking on the next 1 ms tick. The robot reversed 103 mm, raised the held cable, cleared the original snag by 7.15 mm, and placed it on the shelf. A taller barrier snagged the cable again at the safe lift limit, so the robot returned it to the pickup table and opened after the table took its weight. All 52,659 ticks matched after reset, and the unchanged clear-route trajectory matched both obstructed runs until impact. Eleven release tests passed with 367/374 changed production lines and 647/678 regions covered. [Measurements and three native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/serial-cable-snag.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Feeling a cable snag at the gripper

The gripper sensed a snag 120 ms after the cable touched the barrier and carried it to the shelf after a second lift. A light 153 ms rub and a twice-as-fast clear run caused no retreat. A taller barrier forced a return to the pickup table, while a jaw drive failure stopped the carriage within 3.71 mm and the cable fell onto that table. The 700 mm stress case reached the rigid robot during braking and remains outside the cable-only recovery envelope. All 86,037 measured ticks repeated after reset. Twenty-eight Rust tests passed, with 456/464 changed production lines and 854/895 regions covered. [Measured forces and six native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/serial-cable-tactile.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Measuring forces across the folded sheet

A force balance on each cloth panel explains the stiff-grip asymmetry. At 64 native exchanges per millisecond, bending transferred 72.53 mN between the panels, while self-contact friction transferred 1.80 mN. Sampling all native endpoints avoided a large error from observing the elastic force only on the actuator clock. The measured forces balance independently on both panels, although the extreme pad's force split still varies with the physics rate. [Five comparisons and three force replays](measurements/local-manipulation/fold-force-balance.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Updating every public pose in the loaded CPU fleet

For 100,000 moving decks and 1,000 payloads, batching the parent-pose reads lowered complete-tick p95 in two independent campaigns, from 16.89 to 16.28 ms and from 6.52 to 5.41 ms. The faster campaign reached 2.36× real time, leaving a 42.4-fold gap to the 100× target. All 96.48 million public pose pairs, 480,000 native contact points and 240 IMU samples matched across the loaded and sparse controls. All 323 scene tests passed, with 100% changed production line and region coverage. Native constraint velocity resolution remains the largest measured solver cost. [Stage timings, both campaigns and native replay](measurements/local-manipulation/fleet-parent-poses.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Physical damping of a cloth fold

A cloth fold can now dissipate bending vibration at an authored rate. In the controlled test, increasing the damping reduced late tip displacement from 40.99 to 0.14 mm. All 901 states repeated after reset. Seven stiff-gripper trials completed, but at the highest tested rate each jaw briefly lost pressure despite the mean load dividing almost equally. [Measurements and three native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/cloth-bending-damping.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Braking a loaded cable before a rigid jaw hits the barrier

The gripper sensed that the snagged cable was losing supported weight and actively braked the rail. It cleared the 700 mm barrier by 8.05 mm at normal speed and 3.47 mm at twice the speed, then returned the cable to its source. The original stops produced 0.174 and 0.500 N·s of right-jaw impact. The guarded stops produced zero. The clear route and harmless rub matched every command and particle state. Reset reproduced all 148,250 advances. Twenty-one tests passed, and LLVM exercised 395/403 changed production lines and 818/857 regions. [Physical limits, cost and native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/serial-cable-braking.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Keeping a resting payload awake on a moving deck

Forcing 1,000 nearly co-moving payloads to sleep stopped their horizontal motion. Across 40,000 normally supported pair-ticks, carrying the previous pose, twist and reaction forward never matched the continuing solve. Batching the real contact solves preserved all 1,359,760 observed native points. A two-thread guard reduced steady p95 from 9.17 to 6.40 ms, but increased launch-to-landing p95 from 7.31 to 7.57 ms, so the ordinary solver remains in use. The Rust qualification reproduces the 1.122 N·s first landing on a different deck. [Measurements, CPU costs, coverage and native replay](measurements/local-manipulation/fleet-loaded-rest.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Weighing the cable before the robot carries it

The gripper measured 11, 22 and 33 g cables to within 0.02 g during real pickup. A 35 mm sideways probe identified external assistance that made the 22 g cable appear to weigh just 14.35 g. The 33 g cable returned to its source with 2.21 mm rigid clearance. Fast light delivery and a 123 ms rub completed without a false stop. Six refusal cases withheld transport and the post-pickup jaw failure stopped the rail. Reset reproduced 173,525 measured ticks. Twenty-seven release tests passed with 571/580 changed production lines and 1029/1080 regions covered. [Measurements, physical limits and three native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/serial-cable-weigh.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Keeping pressure on a folded sheet with damped pads

Adding physical damping eliminated all 182 simultaneous pressure losses in the 300 ms hold at 250 exchanges per millisecond. Both jaws carried half the sheet's weight. The finest independent comparison still differed by 0.211 mm during release. The first recorded mismatch above 0.1 mm occurred at 2.700 s. Changed production coverage reached 99.25% of lines and 96.59% of regions. [Full measurements and two pressure replays](measurements/local-manipulation/viscous-gripper-pads.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Replaying the local serial gripper from an input journal

The local CPU profile reproduced 595 observations in two fresh processes with one and eight Bevy compute threads. The gripper repeated 200 advancing steps after reset, including contact warm starts and a noisy IMU. A queued wrench survived a paused tick and changed the next solve. A spawned 200 g parcel settled at 1.96205 N before removal. The checked domain is the pinned Linux x86-64 binary and CPU, inline rigid primitives, native joint controllers and IMUs. The next independent replay domain is a resilient midrun restart or another numerical backend. [Commands, measurements and two gz-web-rs replays](measurements/local-manipulation/local-cpu-input-replay.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Reducing collision-search cost during a cloth grasp

Reusing proven clear separation between cloth features reduced the damped gripper's median p95 from 4.80 to 3.75 ms and from 5.92 to 3.69 ms in two campaigns. The spring-only case used less total CPU but its p95 rose about 4–5%. All 32 runs matched the original physical results, and the separate fine-rate check reproduced all 261 refined contacts. Changed production coverage reached 98.06% of lines and 98.91% of regions. [Both campaigns and the native replay](measurements/local-manipulation/cloth-clearance-cost.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Separating jaw release from the sheet hitting the table

The two finest runs released both jaws within 2 µs of one another. Their first table impulses agreed within 0.4%, but by 2.710 s one corner differed by 0.200 mm. Repeating the same jaw motion showed that the cloth and table contact produced most of the subsequent divergence within each matched run. An independent regression fixed a one-tick delay when material reaches a plane or sphere exactly at the tick boundary. [Matched releases, native replays and tests](measurements/local-manipulation/folded-sheet-release-contact.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Rigid and material exchanges on the public gripper clock

The gripper held an 8.4 g folded sheet with 16 rigid/material solves inside each 1 ms Bevy tick. Its jaws carried 41.20 and 41.21 mN and released the sheet onto the bench. At both 16 and 64 exchanges, particle positions and velocities matched independent fine simulations at all 3,400 public endpoints. The maximum torque disagreement was 3.19 × 10⁻¹⁶ N·m. Loaded reset reproduced the pending pad reactions and pickup. Strict release Clippy passed with 474/489 changed production lines and 883/924 regions covered. [Measurements, scope and three native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/public-gripper-exchanges.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Reading range and public poses in the 100,000-deck fleet

Indexing co-moving boxes cut a solid range reading from 16.28 to 0.60 ms with 1,000 loads. With every pose observed, complete-step p95 fell from 40.32 to 16.08 ms. Every measured trajectory, range, IMU and contact point matched through launch and landing. The sensor-free prescribed commit still took 3.09 ms at median against the 0.10 ms budget. A parent-block optimization increased p95, leaving combined pose publication as the next experiment. [Evidence, work boundaries and native gz-web-rs playback](measurements/local-manipulation/fleet-range-publication.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Publishing aligned fleet models and links in one pass

The sensor-free 100,000-deck fleet lowered p95 from 7.09 to 5.15 ms. With 1,000 loads, a real range query and every pose read, p95 fell from 31.35 to 22.31 ms. All native contact, pose and sensor fingerprints matched through launch, landing and reset, and a rotating 8,193-body SDF preserved its link and visual frames. The loaded native step alone still exceeds the full 100× time budget. [Measured boundaries, controls and gz-web-rs replay](measurements/local-manipulation/fleet-flat-hierarchy.md)


## 2026-11-23 — Reading the local robot and resolving a finite material support

The CPU gripper published all six measured coordinates while placing its 200 g parcel. Its full transfer reproduced all 451 visual frames. Correcting sliding displacement halved the incline travel error at every doubling from one to eight material steps. The orange strip then left the finite board and accelerated freely. The finest independent full-sheet runs remained 0.328 mm apart, while the matched 30 ms landing window agreed within 0.028 mm. [Joint sensing and shelf replay](measurements/local-manipulation/cpu-gripper-joint-state.md) · [Material contact, coverage and three replays](measurements/local-manipulation/material-support-edge.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Releasing one of four stationary robot feet

Four 2 kg feet parked together. Removing a pad from under one foot while paused woke only that foot on the next advancing tick. Its body motion and both pad impulses matched continuous dynamics on all 300 resumed ticks, and reset repeated the experiment exactly. Independent headless and renderer runs each added 82 covered scene regions. A cold CLI and gz-web-rs replay preserved all 65 frames. [Measurements, remaining coverage and browser replay](measurements/local-manipulation/parked-foot-cohort.md)

Saved cloth now preserves the sliding equations that generated it. An archived construction reproduced all 100 tested physical endpoints across two surfaces and two loads. Reauthoring it selected the new law and a distinct checkpoint identity. Both feature configurations passed in release mode, and the numerical compatibility change reached 100% changed line and region coverage. [Compatibility and golden data](measurements/local-manipulation/material-support-edge.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Isolating the folded sheet's first sideways impulse

Two independently advanced sheets left the jaws 0.5 µs apart and reached the table 127.5 µs apart. Before touchdown their corners differed by 0.026 mm. The signed sideways table impulses diverged during the first millisecond of impact and the largest measured corner difference reached 0.350 mm. Per-particle table reactions are the next discriminator. [Timed reactions, trajectories and both native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/cloth-incoming-history.md)

## 2026-11-23 — A spherical wrist rests and wakes under load

A balanced 2 kg hand entered the cheaper tier for 300 ticks and woke on the first commanded torque. Its largest positional difference from continuous dynamics was under 3 × 10⁻¹⁵ m. Reloading reproduced all 1,500 states. In a separate strike the first contact impulse and both post-impact readings matched exactly. The eligibility test also caught an inverted hand, an off-center descendant and a rotated slider that could move away from its stop. [Measurements, coverage and native replay](measurements/local-manipulation/hanging-ball-wrist.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Combined release checks for the deterministic local robot candidate

The frozen candidate passed 3,861 headless tests, 2,805 renderer tests and all eight additional physical qualification suites. Both strict release profiles missed the 95% coverage gate. The same binaries passed 65 physical RTX tests, taking GPU physics to 96.30% of lines and 95.57% of regions. The remaining local CLI and scene gaps, absent feature-specific sources and exact per-crate results are recorded in the [release measurements](measurements/local-manipulation/replay-release-gate.md).

## 2026-11-23 — Measuring each corner of the cloth at touchdown

At 1,000 exchanges per millisecond corner 11 touched first. At 2,000 the sheet arrived tilted the opposite way, corner 0 hit 127.5 µs earlier, and contact took 282.5 µs to spread across the bottom plane. The individual readings reproduced the total table impulse on every native step and explain the opposite sideways impulses. The 0.1 mm trajectory target remains open. [Measured contact sequence, coverage and two native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/cloth-table-particles.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Restore the signed Ubuntu bootstrap for hosted robot qualification

The job stopped before compiling the simulator when the Ubuntu snapshot service returned HTTP 500/502 for repository indexes. CI now verifies the archived three signed releases and six exact package indexes before APT uses them. APT authenticates the archives a second time and selects package payloads by their signed records from the Ubuntu archive or the dated snapshot. [Original failed run](https://github.com/gazebosim-rs/gz-sim-rs/actions/runs/45337993773)

A disposable Ubuntu 24.04 instance restored ten intentionally removed packages, including three transitive dependencies, and verified all sixteen direct package versions. The unmodified reference builder compiled all six pinned Gazebo libraries and validated the Zenoh client. With network access disabled, APT rejected a changed release signature, a changed package index and a changed 121,674-byte dependency with its original length. Restoring the correct dependency allowed installation. A Rust regression repeats the dependency check as an unprivileged user in hosted CI. [Package digests and native build receipt](measurements/local-manipulation/signed-ci-bootstrap.json)

Hosted commit `ff4bec9` authenticated and installed the packages in 12 s, rejected a corrupted dependency in 4 s, and built and validated the pinned native client in 2 min 41 s. The later Rust lint stopped on a redundant copy in the spherical wrist test. Commit `692a1ec` removed it and the full default workspace lint passed locally on `68feae4`. [Hosted job and exact step results](https://github.com/gazebosim-rs/gz-sim-rs/actions/runs/45341246092/job/138265151193)

## 2026-11-23 — Read the floor load on each local cloth or cable particle

An SDF material can now publish the normal and friction impulses each particle puts on the floor. Across 1,500 particle readings the worst discrepancy from independent momentum accounting was 5.55 × 10⁻¹⁷ N·s. Removing a separate support, resetting the world and subdividing a public tick preserved the floor identity and integrated readings. Both changed production crates exceeded 98% line and region coverage. [Measurement, portable recording and gz-web-rs replay](measurements/local-manipulation/local-floor-impulses.md)

## 2026-11-23 — The closed gripper tips the folded sheet before release

Both resolutions put the same corner down near 2.521 s and pressed the 8.4 g sheet onto the table with about 1.23 N on average. When the sheet lifted again, the two runs already had opposite spin. A simultaneous hinge-damping trial gave 0.331 mm disagreement versus 0.328 mm with the existing law. Moving the entire experiment by 25 cm changed the trajectory by less than 1 µm. The original solver remains in use. [Measurements, controls and three native replays](measurements/local-manipulation/cloth-preload-study.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Replaying the loaded wrist from its commands

Two queued three-axis torques survived a paused tick and drove the wrist on the next advancing tick. Reset reproduced all 240 repeated steps. Independent processes with one and eight threads produced identical 752-record traces. The ordinary serial transfer model can again replay with its six-coordinate encoder publisher. [Native recordings, command journal and scoped coverage](measurements/local-manipulation/ball-wrist-command-replay.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Profile the 100,000-deck CPU fleet and reduce range-sensor time

Native contact resolution took 1.05 ms at median, shared deck coordinate updates 0.002 ms and Bevy pose publication 0.74 ms. A live bounding check cut p95 range-sensor time from 0.742 to 0.091 ms at the same 640 physical states. The measured fleet ran at 1.24× real time. Whole-step p95 varied between timing methods, while 340 disturbed and prescribed steps and both resets matched the previous physics exactly. [Timings, full-size validation and native replay](measurements/local-manipulation/fleet-native-cost.md)

## 2026-11-23 — A released GPU cuboid moves and tumbles in three dimensions

The four-slider gripper lifted an 80 g eccentric box and opened. One measured off-center impulse sent it 49.507 mm sideways and rotated it 75.689° before the first possible new contact. An independent rigid-body integration agreed within 0.91 nm at the visual origin. The gravity-only control had zero sideways travel and reset reproduced every sampled state. The physical RTX 5090 completed 4,832 observed free steps/s with a 0.239 ms p95. All 35 SDF hardware tests passed and changed production coverage reached 99.57% of lines and 98.64% of regions. Three-dimensional contact response is the next step. [Numerical comparison, native replays and coverage](measurements/local-manipulation/gpu-spatial-flight.md)

## 2026-11-23 — A tumbling GPU cuboid strikes a finite shelf on one corner

The gripper carried the 80 g box 180 mm, opened, and released it above the shelf. Its first corner struck at 2.175338537 s, producing a 0.033538 N s upward impulse and changing its spin about two axes. A separately integrated body agreed within 9.85 ps at touchdown and 18.7 nm through the following 9.66 ms. Lower restitution reduced the rebound, and moving the shelf clear removed the impulse. The next physics step is the following contact and transition to supported rest. [Independent comparison, native replays and scoped coverage](measurements/local-manipulation/gpu-shelf-corner.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Measure four discrete collision volumes in the 100,000-body kinematic scene

The local backend now finds moving bodies inside a requested volume using live collider bounds. In the 100,000-deck scene, four checks fell from 64.53 ms to 2.64 µs at median. All entries and exits matched an exhaustive scan and independent geometry, including a lane which stayed empty. The complete scene reached 3.18× real time with sparse observations and 2.30× with full pose and twist reads, leaving the 100× target open. [Paired timings, controls, coverage and verified gz-web-rs recording](measurements/local-manipulation/kinematic-fleet-gates.md)

## 2026-11-23 — Measure the cost of keeping every fleet pose directly readable

Publishing 100,000 moving bodies took 2.63 ms at median, about 99% of a sparse tick. A write-only control updated the same 500,001 coordinates in 0.383 ms, compared with a 0.10 ms budget for the complete 100× step. Two experiments reduced dirty memory pages but gave mixed timings across readers and CPU core groups. The ordinary publisher remains in use, with a regression for direct edits and change notifications. [Stage timings, memory footprint, coverage, native replay and reproducible trials](measurements/local-manipulation/kinematic-publication-budget.md)
